merging all fields into one slot may turn a nearby integer field into a
handle-derived value. That would be a different verdict even though it
is conservative for aliasing.

| Alternative | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Unbounded explicit enumeration | Same reference semantics, often impractical. Not required by the existing text. |
| Normative widening with the bead's thresholds | Deterministic only with further rules for ordering/stickiness/joins; can reject previously admitted programs. Not acceptable without a capability-preserving lowering for every newly coarse case. |
| Exact symbolic sets and translation-cycle closure (recommended) | Preserves verdicts and handles this witness compactly. Does not claim every SQLite recurrence has this form. |
| Resource/time cap with an explicit unfinished build | Honest operational outcome, not a completed analysis or a source rejection. Remains implementation work. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

Add a dialect K/clarification adjacent to §5.1's K and §5.2's least
fixpoint, in both maintained maps:

```text
The reference bounds INT atoms as above; it imposes no additional
cardinality bound on pointer-offset sets or memory intervals. Implementers
MAY use exact symbolic sets, shared interval relations and SCC
acceleration instead of enumerating represented offsets or slots.
All reference sets and byte/representation-width distinctions must be
preserved for verdicts, candidate sets and dispatch domains. A coarser
summary is not the reference result merely because it is sound for
aliasing. An unfinished analysis reports an unfinished build with the
rule and exhausted implementation resource, not a source rejection,
completed verdict or run-time fault for a valid input.

In a pure modular-translation inclusion SCC, if every cycle transfer is
an unfiltered fixed displacement at one bit width W and preserves one
base/provenance coordinate, close the generated displacements as a
subgroup of Z/(2^W). For a single self-cycle of stride s, each seed a
denotes the coset a modulo gcd(s,2^W). Union distinct seed cosets. For
multiple vertices, retain vertex-specific path displacements; do not
merge their seed coordinates. Apply the same rule to a memory relation
only when every transfer preserves the complete storage interval and
representation width and all PT6–PT8 read/overlap results are identical
to the represented individual slots. Retain every ROOT and FN alternative
explicitly. Otherwise use the original rules or another exact method.
PT2's pointer-index width and preserved high pointer bits still apply;
this rule does not replace them by unrestricted full-pointer addition.
```

Add to §24:

```text
Analysis representation/acceleration is implementation policy; the
default result is the exact §5 least fixpoint, including existing K=4096
INT abstraction. Time or memory caps do not alter that result. A report
of unfinished analysis keeps the remaining implementation obligation open.
```

This recommends an exact acceleration and explicitly **declines new
verdict-affecting bounds**. If council instead wants a new abstract
domain, it must supply canonical joins, evaluation order independence,
ROOT/partial-access rules and a lowering that preserves all programs
previously in scope; the current bead does not establish those properties.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

An exact representation clarification changes no materialised program,
ISA object/certificate or catalog bytes. A dialect publication still
gets its required revision/mapping identity. A threshold widening could
change descriptors, dispatch domains, diagnostics and emitted object
hashes, so cannot masquerade as a local plugin optimization. Plugin and
independent dialect checker should compare normalized exact results on
small-width cases, not implementation data-structure counts.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Enumerate all W=8, stride-8 cases above and compare their symbolic cosets;
W=64 is a symbolic cardinality test, never a 2^61-element allocation.
Test stride 0, stride 1, multiple seeds, a cycle with a filter (must not
use the pure-cycle rule), and GEP with I(a)<P(a). Memory vectors include
same-width full copies, overlapping partial copies and adjacent handle/
integer fields; retain PT6's non-handle top and ROOT distinctions. Check
identical verdicts independent of worklist order. Mathematical cosets are
checked by [contract-verification](2026-11-02-contract-verification.md).
The SQLite exact fixpoint and production gate remain unverified here.

## Release and after acceptance

Recommend a **dialect K clarification on the current 0.2/0.3 tracks**,
not an ISA-0.10 dependency. As stated, 5u3 is primarily an implementation
scalability request. After acceptance, add the finite vectors and let the
plugin owner establish exact convergence on both LLVM majors and the
full rung-4 closure. No new source restriction is authorized by this note.

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1020:| PT1 | `alloca`, global `@G`, allocation-site call | AV ∋ (X, 0); zero-filled initial extents are seeded as above |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1021:| PT2 | `getelementptr T, ptr p, idx…` | uses P(a), I(a) and the complete finite-width computation of §16.4, also for constant expressions: sequential indices are sign-extended/truncated to I(a), scaling and offset accumulation wrap modulo 2^I(a), and addition preserves pointer bits above I(a). Zero accumulated offset copies AV(p). For every (X,o), include the image of all represented base addresses and index values (unknown base bits range over patterns consistent with DataLayout and X's alignment), using integer/residue-class/`*` offset unions. Unbounded mathematical addition is not a substitute. If an address may leave X, include (EXT, `*`) and the non-handle top flag; PT6 propagates this unknown-address alternative. The unknown-address alternative does not by itself establish host escape (§6.4): retain the original base provenance for that test; actual host/unknown origins keep their restrictions. ROOT provenance and §6.2 restrictions remain explicit. Array dimensions determine stride, never an index bound |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1022:| PT3 | `phi`, `select` (value operands), `freeze`, `addrspacecast`; `bitcast` | the first four copy (union). A bitcast copies lane by lane only when source and destination have identical lane counts and per-lane representation widths, treating a scalar as one lane. Otherwise use the virtual N21 bitcast expansion of §12.6: pack source-lane bit patterns into its prescribed bit string in DataLayout order, then resegment at the destination representation width. Compute output AV through PT4/PT5 transfers of that expansion; finite INT lane sets denote their full Cartesian product, and K applies per resulting lane. Never copy a source-lane INT atom as the value of a packed destination scalar. Unknown bits propagate non-handle top through those transfers. Explicit object and ROOT provenance follows the same expansion and remains subject to §6.2; top never substitutes for ROOT. Apply also to constant expressions and fixed-vscale forms during S2 before verdicts, candidate sets, shapes and dispatch domains freeze. Direct finite bit-string evaluation is equivalent; no LLVM optimisation is required |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1023:| PT4 | `ptrtoint`, `inttoptr`, `zext`/`sext`/`trunc` | copy only bit-preserving conversions. Otherwise apply the actual source/destination-width bit-vector conversion (§16.4): ptrtoint/inttoptr zero-extend or truncate at P(a), sext sign-extends. Use PT2’s represented-address image construction, retaining originating object and ROOT provenance. A narrowed representation that cannot be reassociated with its original object adds (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤; numerical uncertainty is not host escape. Apply also to constant expressions |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1024:| PT5 | `add`/`sub` of a value with pointer atoms and an `INT` set | computes address bits modulo 2^W at the integer operation’s width W, never unbounded offset addition. Relative displacements from the originating base are modular too: zero net displacement represents that base again, including after an inttoptr truncation to P(a). Use PT2’s represented-address image construction at W bits (full-width arithmetic, no preserved high GEP bits), retaining original object and ROOT provenance. Keep modular displacement alternatives even outside the allocation, and additionally include (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤ when an image may leave it. Residue classes and `*` take the same finite-width image; an unrepresentable image retains (X, `*`) for each originating X plus (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤. Other integer operations on pointer atoms use that conservative image. Widening a pointer-derived integer by zext/sext uses that conservative image too (fixed reference fallback); ptrtoint zero-extension retains its base representation. Numerical uncertainty does not itself establish host escape (PT2). The rules apply also to constant expressions, before candidate/dispatch domains freeze; on finite `INT` sets, every LLVM integer operation, cast and `icmp` is evaluated elementwise; with x ⊤ and a constant m, `and x, m` gives the submasks of m and `urem x, m` gives {0, …, m−1} (each still subject to K); a range attribute or `!range` metadata removed by N3/N4 is not a bound on materialised execution and MUST NOT restrict any verdict, candidate set, descriptor shape or dispatch domain. Only bounds independently established by materialised instructions restrict the set; otherwise retain ⊤ and apply the ordinary operation rules, including finite masks/remainders |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1025:| PT6 | `load T, ptr p` | for each (X,o) ∈ AV(p): memory atoms carry their storage interval and representation width; copy a whole-value atom only for that identical interval with a compatible representation. A known immutable byte image decodes exactly the accessed bytes using DataLayout and T (including initialisers). For a partial, spanning or overlapping access whose result bits are not represented exactly, the non-handle value is ⊤, never the union of component INT values. X=EXT gives ⊤. A non-handle top flag on p contributes the same unknown-address load alternative even when explicit object offsets overlap no known slot; it MUST NOT be dropped. ROOT provenance remains explicit and §6.2's partial-handle restrictions still apply. For a possibly valid load wholly contained in a zero-filled initial extent, include the all-zero value of the load’s representation and width, unioned with values from every overlapping store or copy under these interval rules. PT8 propagates these initial byte possibilities when copying memory. Zero contributes no ROOT. Region inputs, replayed caller bytes and other nonzero-initialised contents retain their specified sources; this rule does not replace them with zeros |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1026:| PT7 | `store v, ptr p` | retain each written interval and representation width; partial writes overlapping a larger load participate in PT6's conservative fallback. AV(X, o + d_k) ⊇ AV(v_k) for each (X, o) ∈ AV(p) and each lane or field k at byte offset d_k |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1027:| PT8 | memory copy (`llvm.memcpy`/`memmove`/`memcpy.inline`, `__bennett_rt_memmove`) of (dst, src, len); memory fill (`llvm.memset`, `__bennett_rt_memset`) of (dst, v, len) | copy: propagate zero-byte initial images over the copied byte ranges, including their symbolic extents; for each slot (Y, s) of each (Y, o_s) ∈ AV(src) and (Z, o_d) ∈ AV(dst): when offsets and len are constants and the atom's entire storage interval [s, s + size(atom)) lies in the copied range, copy its atom and representation width to (Z, o_d + s − o_s). Every partially overlapping atom instead contributes non-handle ⊤ and explicit ROOT provenance to the affected destination interval (unknown offsets/len use `*`); do not copy the whole component INT. Fill: every slot in [o_d, o_d + len) of each (Z, o_d) ∈ AV(dst) (all of Z when an offset or len is not constant) gets the `INT` atoms of the byte v repeated to the slot's width (⊤ when v is not constant) |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1028:| PT9 | direct call of G, or indirect call with candidate G (§14.2) | AV(parameter i of G) ⊇ AV(argument i); AV(result) ⊇ the union of AV over G's `ret` operands; `byval` arguments copy the pointee's slots into the callee's copy object |

Constant in-range extraction copies its lane; constant out-of-range extraction gives the element type’s zero. Dynamic extraction unions every source lane selected by a represented index, plus zero iff the index set includes an unsigned value >= L (including top when 2^K > L). No zero is added if every represented index is in range; zero adds no ROOT. In-range insertion replaces the selected lane; out-of-range insertion preserves the vector. Dynamic insertion includes each old lane unless it is certainly replaced, and the inserted value whenever that lane can be selected. Valid shuffle indices and aggregate paths move atoms unchanged (handle-table reads, audit B-23) |

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6789:## 13. Floating point (arith contract)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6795:### 13.1 Soft-float rewrite (rules N19, N32)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6823:| `llvm.sqrt`, `llvm.floor`, `llvm.ceil`, `llvm.trunc`, `llvm.round`, `llvm.roundeven`, `llvm.rint`, `llvm.nearbyint`, `llvm.canonicalize` | `C @__bennett_sf_<name>_B(C)` | bitcast |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6834:For each such name fmt and each B of §13.1, N32 MUST emit `iN @__bennett_sf_convert_to_<fmt>_B(C_B, i32 R, i1 Sat)` for to-arbitrary (format MDString removed, rounding encoded by §13.5, immediate saturation retained), and `C_B @__bennett_sf_convert_from_<fmt>_B(iN)` for from-arbitrary; fmt is spelled exactly as its case-sensitive MDString, N is its listed bit width, and results are integer bits / bitcast B respectively. Finite conversions use the named format's rounding, saturation and nonfinite rules with canonical NaNs; N1 defines a source-poison conversion result as zero bits, including unsaturated out-of-range FP4/FP6 inputs. A missing APFloat lowering is an implementation obligation, never permission to reject a valid named format or emit an unresolved numerical placeholder.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6836:**Static instantiation request v1.** The driver MUST collect required concrete arith instances before the final link and supply a UTF-8 JSON object with exactly `schema: "bennett-arith-instantiations-1"`, `llvm_major` (integer), `target_triple` (string), `data_layout` (LLVM string), and `entries` (array); a conversion entry has exactly `op: "convert_to" | "convert_from"`, `format` (the MDString above), and `base` (B suffix), and a powi entry has exactly `op: "powi"`, `base`, and `exponent_bits` (integer K, 1..8388608). A powi entry requests both `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>(C_B, iK)` and `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>_rm(C_B, iK, i32 R)` (the suffix `_rm` is last); a conversion entry requests its exact signature above. Duplicate entries denote one instance; canonical request bytes are JCS with entries sorted by (op, base, format or empty string, exponent_bits or zero).
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6849:intervenes. For canonical temporary slots, emit ss_push(8L,8), then limb
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6863:observable flags or exceptions (except §13.5); **every NaN result is the format's canonical NaN**
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6866:the f64 canonical NaN in the high double and +0 in the low); `rem` is C `fmod` (exact, the sign of
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6872:operand; two: the canonical NaN) with −0 < +0; `minimum`/`maximum` are IEEE 754-2019 (either NaN:
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6873:the canonical NaN) with −0 < +0; `minimumnum`/`maximumnum` are IEEE 754-2019 minimumNumber/
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6876:nearest even; `canonicalize` maps every NaN to the canonical NaN and is the identity otherwise;
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6887:For these guarantees, a finite canonical double-double is a nonoverlapping pair (h,l) of binary64 values with h = roundTiesToEven(h+l) and l the exact residual (positive zero when the residual is zero), zero represented as (signed zero,+0); the result MUST be the canonical pair whose exact sum is nearest the exact mathematical result (or its directed neighbour for §13.5), with ties to the even low-component significand, using the high significand when the low component is zero, and NaN/inf represented in the high component with low +0. Overflow is determined by rounding with an unbounded high exponent first and then applying binary64's high-component exponent limit with IEEE overflow outcomes for the active mode (the finite maximum or infinity as appropriate); underflow uses tininess after rounding of the exact pair sum, with subnormal threshold 2^-1022.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6891:Constrained operations covered by these guarantees MUST derive invalid/divbyzero/overflow/underflow/inexact from the mathematical operation and final rounding, not the status of an inaccurate APFloat fallback or an intermediate high/low operation; for sqrt/fma/conversion, inexact means the final pair's exact sum differs from the exact finite result, and underflow requires both tininess after rounding and inexactness. Rounding-to-integral follows the named IEEE operation: rint/lrint/llrint signal inexact on a nonintegral finite operand, nearbyint suppresses inexact, and floor/ceil/trunc/round/roundeven/lround/llround do not raise inexact merely for discarding a fractional part. Other PPC operations retain their named major-specific APFloat semantics subject to the explicit canonical-NaN rules above.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6893:### 13.2 Vector floating point
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6899:### 13.3 Formats beyond binary32 and binary64
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6904:### 13.4 Transcendentals and libm intrinsics (N33)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6926:canonical encoding, with signed zeros preserved where the operation requires.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:6949:**Accuracy envelope.** For each finite mathematical result of a §13.4 own-format carrier transcendental, the implementation MUST return a B value within one local ulp of that exact result, over the entire finite input domain; local ulp is the spacing of B's adjacent finite values bracketing the exact result, taking an exactly representable result as the lower endpoint (the upper endpoint at the largest finite value), with the least positive subnormal spacing at zero or in the subnormal interval. For ppcf128 use §13.1's ordered canonical-pair values. Special values, domain errors, poles, signed zeros and overflow results follow the named operation's mathematical/IEEE semantics rather than this finite-result envelope. At overflow the result is the correctly rounded overflow outcome for the requested mode. This is an error bound, not a general correct-rounding claim.

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:8703:**`indirectbr-two-blocks`.** The two address-taken blocks have values B(f)=4096 and B(f)+1=4097. The address object has two bytes. An IN(64,zext) pointer selects the first or second block and returns 7 or 9. Any other address reaches fault 4. The unused ptrtoint of the block object keeps its address explicitly in the reference closure. Success counts are wrapper 2 + address rows 2 + compare path 3 or 6 + return 2: 9 and 12 rows. No-match executes only the wrapper call, address rows 2, compares 6 and fault 1, for 10 rows; wrapper retval never executes.

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:970:### 5.1 Abstract objects and atoms
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1003:holds more than **K = 4096** `INT` atoms they are dropped and t is set (a normative constant: it

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1009:### 5.2 Rules
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1017:The analysis is the least fixpoint of the following rules over AV. "Copies" means AV(result) ⊇
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1024:| PT1 | `alloca`, global `@G`, allocation-site call | AV ∋ (X, 0); zero-filled initial extents are seeded as above |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1025:| PT2 | `getelementptr T, ptr p, idx…` | uses P(a), I(a) and the complete finite-width computation of §16.4, also for constant expressions: sequential indices are sign-extended/truncated to I(a), scaling and offset accumulation wrap modulo 2^I(a), and addition preserves pointer bits above I(a). Zero accumulated offset copies AV(p). For every (X,o), include the image of all represented base addresses and index values (unknown base bits range over patterns consistent with DataLayout and X's alignment), using integer/residue-class/`*` offset unions. Unbounded mathematical addition is not a substitute. If an address may leave X, include (EXT, `*`) and the non-handle top flag; PT6 propagates this unknown-address alternative. The unknown-address alternative does not by itself establish host escape (§6.4): retain the original base provenance for that test; actual host/unknown origins keep their restrictions. ROOT provenance and §6.2 restrictions remain explicit. Array dimensions determine stride, never an index bound |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1026:| PT3 | `phi`, `select` (value operands), `freeze`, `addrspacecast`; `bitcast` | the first four copy (union). A bitcast copies lane by lane only when source and destination have identical lane counts and per-lane representation widths, treating a scalar as one lane. Otherwise use the virtual N21 bitcast expansion of §12.6: pack source-lane bit patterns into its prescribed bit string in DataLayout order, then resegment at the destination representation width. Compute output AV through PT4/PT5 transfers of that expansion; finite INT lane sets denote their full Cartesian product, and K applies per resulting lane. Never copy a source-lane INT atom as the value of a packed destination scalar. Unknown bits propagate non-handle top through those transfers. Explicit object and ROOT provenance follows the same expansion and remains subject to §6.2; top never substitutes for ROOT. Apply also to constant expressions and fixed-vscale forms during S2 before verdicts, candidate sets, shapes and dispatch domains freeze. Direct finite bit-string evaluation is equivalent; no LLVM optimisation is required |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1027:| PT4 | `ptrtoint`, `inttoptr`, `zext`/`sext`/`trunc` | copy only bit-preserving conversions. Otherwise apply the actual source/destination-width bit-vector conversion (§16.4): ptrtoint/inttoptr zero-extend or truncate at P(a), sext sign-extends. Use PT2’s represented-address image construction, retaining originating object and ROOT provenance. A narrowed representation that cannot be reassociated with its original object adds (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤; numerical uncertainty is not host escape. Apply also to constant expressions |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1028:| PT5 | `add`/`sub` of a value with pointer atoms and an `INT` set | computes address bits modulo 2^W at the integer operation’s width W, never unbounded offset addition. Relative displacements from the originating base are modular too: zero net displacement represents that base again, including after an inttoptr truncation to P(a). Use PT2’s represented-address image construction at W bits (full-width arithmetic, no preserved high GEP bits), retaining original object and ROOT provenance. Keep modular displacement alternatives even outside the allocation, and additionally include (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤ when an image may leave it. Residue classes and `*` take the same finite-width image; an unrepresentable image retains (X, `*`) for each originating X plus (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤. Other integer operations on pointer atoms use that conservative image. Widening a pointer-derived integer by zext/sext uses that conservative image too (fixed reference fallback); ptrtoint zero-extension retains its base representation. Numerical uncertainty does not itself establish host escape (PT2). The rules apply also to constant expressions, before candidate/dispatch domains freeze; on finite `INT` sets, every LLVM integer operation, cast and `icmp` is evaluated elementwise; with x ⊤ and a constant m, `and x, m` gives the submasks of m and `urem x, m` gives {0, …, m−1} (each still subject to K); a range attribute or `!range` metadata removed by N3/N4 is not a bound on materialised execution and MUST NOT restrict any verdict, candidate set, descriptor shape or dispatch domain. Only bounds independently established by materialised instructions restrict the set; otherwise retain ⊤ and apply the ordinary operation rules, including finite masks/remainders |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1029:| PT6 | `load T, ptr p` | for each (X,o) ∈ AV(p): memory atoms carry their storage interval and representation width; copy a whole-value atom only for that identical interval with a compatible representation. A known immutable byte image decodes exactly the accessed bytes using DataLayout and T (including initialisers). For a partial, spanning or overlapping access whose result bits are not represented exactly, the non-handle value is ⊤, never the union of component INT values. X=EXT gives ⊤. A non-handle top flag on p contributes the same unknown-address load alternative even when explicit object offsets overlap no known slot; it MUST NOT be dropped. ROOT provenance remains explicit and §6.2's partial-handle restrictions still apply. For a possibly valid load wholly contained in a zero-filled initial extent, include the all-zero value of the load’s representation and width, unioned with values from every overlapping store or copy under these interval rules. PT8 propagates these initial byte possibilities when copying memory. Zero contributes no ROOT. Region inputs, replayed caller bytes and other nonzero-initialised contents retain their specified sources; this rule does not replace them with zeros |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1030:| PT7 | `store v, ptr p` | retain each written interval and representation width; partial writes overlapping a larger load participate in PT6's conservative fallback. AV(X, o + d_k) ⊇ AV(v_k) for each (X, o) ∈ AV(p) and each lane or field k at byte offset d_k |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1031:| PT8 | memory copy (`llvm.memcpy`/`memmove`/`memcpy.inline`, `__bennett_rt_memmove`) of (dst, src, len); memory fill (`llvm.memset`, `__bennett_rt_memset`) of (dst, v, len) | copy: propagate zero-byte initial images over the copied byte ranges, including their symbolic extents; for each slot (Y, s) of each (Y, o_s) ∈ AV(src) and (Z, o_d) ∈ AV(dst): when offsets and len are constants and the atom's entire storage interval [s, s + size(atom)) lies in the copied range, copy its atom and representation width to (Z, o_d + s − o_s). Every partially overlapping atom instead contributes non-handle ⊤ and explicit ROOT provenance to the affected destination interval (unknown offsets/len use `*`); do not copy the whole component INT. Fill: every slot in [o_d, o_d + len) of each (Z, o_d) ∈ AV(dst) (all of Z when an offset or len is not constant) gets the `INT` atoms of the byte v repeated to the slot's width (⊤ when v is not constant) |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1032:| PT9 | direct call of G, or indirect call with candidate G (§14.2) | AV(parameter i of G) ⊇ AV(argument i); AV(result) ⊇ the union of AV over G's `ret` operands; `byval` arguments copy the pointee's slots into the callee's copy object |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1033:| PT10 | `insertelement`/`extractelement`/`shufflevector`/`insertvalue`/`extractvalue` | extraction/insertion use N15’s total unsigned index semantics. Constant in-range extraction copies its lane; constant out-of-range extraction gives the element type’s zero. Dynamic extraction unions every source lane selected by a represented index, plus zero iff the index set includes an unsigned value >= L (including top when 2^K > L). No zero is added if every represented index is in range; zero adds no ROOT. In-range insertion replaces the selected lane; out-of-range insertion preserves the vector. Dynamic insertion includes each old lane unless it is certainly replaced, and the inserted value whenever that lane can be selected. Valid shuffle indices and aggregate paths move atoms unchanged (handle-table reads, audit B-23) |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1034:| PT11 | a parameter of a function with a host caller, a device entry, or a function whose address host code uses | AV ⊇ {(EXT, `*`)} for `ptr` parameters, ⊤ for integer parameters, plus the atoms of the device-side arguments by PT9; for a pointer parameter i of a device entry E, also the candidate atom ROOT(E, i) of §6.6 |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1035:| PT12 | a `qalloc`/`qborrow` call; a call of a function with no definition that resolves to a host call (§4.5) or is trapped; an `atomicrmw`/`cmpxchg` (before N9) | `qalloc`/`qborrow`: AV ∋ ROOT(site) (§6.5). An undefined callee: the result is ⊤ and every slot reachable from its pointer arguments gets ⊤. Before domains freeze, PT12 MUST apply PT6 to the old value, the PT5 transfers of the full N9 update, and PT7 to the actual stored value, with cmpxchg returning its old-value and success fields and storing the selected new-or-old value. [deps: DLT-5.2-P1, DLT-5.2-P6, DLT-5.2-P7, DLT-5.2-P8, DLT-5.2-P13, DLT-12.2-P11, ISA-4.1, ISA-4.2] |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1042:**PT12 N9 update witnesses (DB/DN/DS; non-normative).** The global starts at 1. Xor by 2 alternates 1 and 3 in the flow-insensitive least fixpoint; a single execution allocates width 3. Cmpxchg against 1 first succeeds and writes 3; subsequent abstract iterations include failure on old 3. Against 9 it always fails and leaves 1. Native LLVM 18/23 materialised O0/O3 checks old/success, actual width, clean free, liveness and code 0.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1112:**PT12 spelling witnesses (non-normative).** Explicit address spaces, volatile/weak qualifiers, syncscope and alignment retain the same N9 value effects and native materialised outcomes; the pointer operand keeps its address-space type in the generated load and store.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1160:PT12 checked ROOT propagation: `make` returns the fresh qalloc ROOT to its caller;
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1182:same derivation. The PT8 companion copies the stack image to a mutable global
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1270:**PT3 geometry-changing bitcast witnesses (DB/DN/DS).** On this little-endian

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1432:**PT6 byte reconstruction witnesses.** Immutable bytes [1,1] decode as
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1479:overlapping writes still participate in PT6 (a fill does not erase them).

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:4775:| N9 | `atomic` and `volatile` on loads and stores; `fence`; `atomicrmw`; `cmpxchg` | plain load/store; fence deleted; `atomicrmw op ptr p, T v` → `%nz.l.r = load T, ptr p`, `%nz.v.r = <op> T %nz.l.r, v` for `add`/`sub`/`and`/`or`/`xor` (for `xchg`: v; `min`/`max`/`umin`/`umax`: `icmp` + `select` named `%nz.k.r`, `%nz.v.r`; FP `fadd`/`fsub`: ordinary `fadd`/`fsub` then N32; `fmax`/`fmin`, `fmaximum`/`fminimum`, `fmaximumnum`/`fminimumnum`: respectively `llvm.maxnum`/`llvm.minnum`, `llvm.maximum`/`llvm.minimum`, `llvm.maximumnum`/`llvm.minimumnum` then N32; `nand`: `%nz.a.r = and T %nz.l.r, v`, then `%nz.v.r = xor T %nz.a.r, -1` (these two logical instructions, in this order, without flags); `uinc_wrap`: k=`icmp uge old,v`, i=`add old,1`, new=`select k,0,i`; `udec_wrap`: k=`icmp eq old,0`, g=`icmp ugt old,v`, w=`or k,g`, d=`sub old,1`, new=`select w,v,d`; `usub_cond`/`usub_sat`: k=`icmp uge old,v`, s=`sub old,v`, new=`select k,s,old` / `select k,s,0`. spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6803:### 13.1 Soft-float rewrite (rules N19, N32)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6831:| `llvm.sqrt`, `llvm.floor`, `llvm.ceil`, `llvm.trunc`, `llvm.round`, `llvm.roundeven`, `llvm.rint`, `llvm.nearbyint`, `llvm.canonicalize` | `C @__bennett_sf_<name>_B(C)` | bitcast |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6842:For each such name fmt and each B of §13.1, N32 MUST emit `iN @__bennett_sf_convert_to_<fmt>_B(C_B, i32 R, i1 Sat)` for to-arbitrary (format MDString removed, rounding encoded by §13.5, immediate saturation retained), and `C_B @__bennett_sf_convert_from_<fmt>_B(iN)` for from-arbitrary; fmt is spelled exactly as its case-sensitive MDString, N is its listed bit width, and results are integer bits / bitcast B respectively. Finite conversions use the named format's rounding, saturation and nonfinite rules with canonical NaNs; N1 defines a source-poison conversion result as zero bits, including unsaturated out-of-range FP4/FP6 inputs. A missing APFloat lowering is an implementation obligation, never permission to reject a valid named format or emit an unresolved numerical placeholder.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6844:**Static instantiation request v1.** The driver MUST collect required concrete arith instances before the final link and supply a UTF-8 JSON object with exactly `schema: "bennett-arith-instantiations-1"`, `llvm_major` (integer), `target_triple` (string), `data_layout` (LLVM string), and `entries` (array); a conversion entry has exactly `op: "convert_to" | "convert_from"`, `format` (the MDString above), and `base` (B suffix), and a powi entry has exactly `op: "powi"`, `base`, and `exponent_bits` (integer K, 1..8388608). A powi entry requests both `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>(C_B, iK)` and `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>_rm(C_B, iK, i32 R)` (the suffix `_rm` is last); a conversion entry requests its exact signature above. Duplicate entries denote one instance; canonical request bytes are JCS with entries sorted by (op, base, format or empty string, exponent_bits or zero).
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6857:intervenes. For canonical temporary slots, emit ss_push(8L,8), then limb
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6871:observable flags or exceptions (except §13.5); **every NaN result is the format's canonical NaN**
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6874:the f64 canonical NaN in the high double and +0 in the low); `rem` is C `fmod` (exact, the sign of
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6880:operand; two: the canonical NaN) with −0 < +0; `minimum`/`maximum` are IEEE 754-2019 (either NaN:
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6881:the canonical NaN) with −0 < +0; `minimumnum`/`maximumnum` are IEEE 754-2019 minimumNumber/
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6884:nearest even; `canonicalize` maps every NaN to the canonical NaN and is the identity otherwise;
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6895:For these guarantees, a finite canonical double-double is a nonoverlapping pair (h,l) of binary64 values with h = roundTiesToEven(h+l) and l the exact residual (positive zero when the residual is zero), zero represented as (signed zero,+0); the result MUST be the canonical pair whose exact sum is nearest the exact mathematical result (or its directed neighbour for §13.5), with ties to the even low-component significand, using the high significand when the low component is zero, and NaN/inf represented in the high component with low +0. Overflow is determined by rounding with an unbounded high exponent first and then applying binary64's high-component exponent limit with IEEE overflow outcomes for the active mode (the finite maximum or infinity as appropriate); underflow uses tininess after rounding of the exact pair sum, with subnormal threshold 2^-1022.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6899:Constrained operations covered by these guarantees MUST derive invalid/divbyzero/overflow/underflow/inexact from the mathematical operation and final rounding, not the status of an inaccurate APFloat fallback or an intermediate high/low operation; for sqrt/fma/conversion, inexact means the final pair's exact sum differs from the exact finite result, and underflow requires both tininess after rounding and inexactness. Rounding-to-integral follows the named IEEE operation: rint/lrint/llrint signal inexact on a nonintegral finite operand, nearbyint suppresses inexact, and floor/ceil/trunc/round/roundeven/lround/llround do not raise inexact merely for discarding a fractional part. Other PPC operations retain their named major-specific APFloat semantics subject to the explicit canonical-NaN rules above.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6901:### 13.2 Vector floating point
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6907:### 13.3 Formats beyond binary32 and binary64
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6912:### 13.4 Transcendentals and libm intrinsics (N33)
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6934:canonical encoding, with signed zeros preserved where the operation requires.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:6957:**Accuracy envelope.** For each finite mathematical result of a §13.4 own-format carrier transcendental, the implementation MUST return a B value within one local ulp of that exact result, over the entire finite input domain; local ulp is the spacing of B's adjacent finite values bracketing the exact result, taking an exactly representable result as the lower endpoint (the upper endpoint at the largest finite value), with the least positive subnormal spacing at zero or in the subnormal interval. For ppcf128 use §13.1's ordered canonical-pair values. Special values, domain errors, poles, signed zeros and overflow results follow the named operation's mathematical/IEEE semantics rather than this finite-result envelope. At overflow the result is the correctly rounded overflow outcome for the requested mode. This is an error bound, not a general correct-rounding claim.

An IN(64,zext) pointer selects the first or second block and returns 7 or 9. Any other address reaches fault 4. The unused ptrtoint of the block object keeps its address explicitly in the reference closure. Success counts are wrapper 2 + address rows 2 + compare path 3 or 6 + return 2: 9 and 12 rows. No-match executes only the wrapper call, address rows 2, compares 6 and fault 1, for 10 rows; wrapper retval never executes.

---
name: beads
description: Use when working in a repository that uses bd or Beads for durable project task tracking, issue dependencies, blocker management, multi-session handoff, or shared work memory. Trigger when the user asks to find ready work, claim or close tasks, create follow-up work, inspect blockers, recover project context, or choose between local planning and persistent project tracking.

# Beads

Use Beads as the shared project task system. Local plans, scratch files, and personal memories are useful, but they are not the durable source of truth for project work.

## First Step

Run:

```bash
bd prime
```

If that prints nothing, check whether the repository has an active Beads workspace:

```bash
bd where
```

## Preferred Route

Use the `bd` CLI when shell access is available. It is the most compact and direct Beads interface.

## Core CLI Workflow

1. Find work:

```bash
bd ready
bd list --status=open
bd list --status=in_progress
```

2. Inspect before editing:

```bash
bd show <id>
```

3. Claim work atomically:

```bash
bd update <id> --claim
```

4. Create durable follow-up work when implementation reveals new tasks:

```bash
bd create "Short title" --description="Why this exists and what needs to be done" --type=task --priority=2
```

5. Close completed work:

```bash
bd close <id> --reason="Completed"
```

## What Belongs In Beads

Use Beads for:

- shared project tasks
- blockers and dependencies
- discovered follow-up work
- work that must survive thread reset, compaction, or handoff
- status that another person or agent should be able to resume

Use agent-local planning tools only for the current turn's execution checklist. Do not treat them as shared project state.

## Rules

- Do not create markdown TODO files as the source of truth when Beads is available.
- Do not use `bd edit`; it opens an interactive editor. Use `bd update` flags instead.
- Prefer `--json` when parsing `bd` output programmatically.
- If hooks are installed, `bd prime` may already be injected. Run it manually when context is missing.
- Do not auto-close or mutate tasks unless the work is actually complete.

exec
/bin/bash -lc "sed -n '1,180p' spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md && sed -n '950,1040p' spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md && sed -n '1450,1675p' spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md" in /home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-dialect-spec
exec
/bin/bash -lc 'nl -ba ../bennett-isa-spec/docs/notes/2026-11-02-contract-5u3.md && cat ../bennett-isa-spec/docs/notes/2026-11-02-contract-verification.md' in /home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-dialect-spec

# Bennett LLVM Dialect — Specification, draft 0.2.12

**Date:** 2026-11-01. **Status:** draft 0.2.12 accepted by the sol seat in round 11 (2026-11-01; 0 blocking; gate 2893/2893).
This draft supersedes 0.2.11, accepted by the council's sol and codex seats in round 10
(2026-10-31; gate 2834/2834), retained at git `eaad067` with accepted-text SHA-256
`05101a18e04098cfca5fdfb52edc9d9027723455adf2569da2fa49ef7a47c7c1`.
Its pre-acceptance freeze receipt records spec SHA-256
`67157631837887fabb412b762d571157f71dbb79bb9ff13331ec692ce7ad82ba`.
Accepted 0.2.10 and its SHA are retained by `errata-0.2.10.md`; its text is unchanged.
The grading target remains Bennett ISA **0.8**. [deps: DLT-front-P2, DLT-1.3, DLT-23]

**Versioning.** Dialect 0.2.12: markers 0.2, handles 0.2.8, regions 0.2.8, oracle-abi 0.2.4, tdl 0.2.4, effects 0.2.4, norm 0.2.8, map 0.2.9, asm 0.2, platform 0.2.1, driver 0.2.1, corpus 0.2; targets ISA 0.8. [deps: DLT-front-P2, DLT-23]
The **full version string** (`0.2.12`) is the compatibility unit (§23): drafts are not compatible with each other. [deps: DLT-front-P2, DLT-23]
The compatible track MUST retain existing marker signatures, canonical map and externally visible reference behaviour while clarifying inherited rules and supplying closure evidence. [deps: DLT-front-P2, DLT-3.2, DLT-16, DLT-23]
Declared dependency markers are retained for historical context; the council withdrew the requirement on 2026-10-31 (CLAUDE.md rule 12).
ISA 0.9 adoption remains in the separate 0.3 track described by `outline-0.3.md`, D15–D19; this draft does not adopt those operations.

**Standing rule (CLAUDE.md rule 10; council rubric S0).** This document narrows no capability
that the north star promises (its thesis, §3–§5 and every rung of the §8 demo ladder; Grover
over unmodified SQLite is the core). It rejects at compile time only what the north star itself
requires to be rejected or what a maintainer decision settles; every such rejection is a row of
§22.1 citing the clause or decision. A deferral (§24) postpones mechanics only and states the
capability it keeps. Where ISA 0.8 cannot express a promised capability in some case, the
dialect still specifies the whole mechanism, states the ISA 0.8 realisation that exists for the
other cases, and names a blocker (§25) whose verdict is `blocked:`, never `reject:`.

**Sources.** North star §0, §3, §4, §5, §8; the legality spike and its findings
(`docs/spikes/2026-10-28-legality-findings.md`, decisions 1–4); the narrowing audit (row ids
D1–D25, B-01–B-55, O-01–O-08, G-12–G-16, ISA-1–ISA-11 are cited unchanged); the rung-4 spike
(`docs/spikes/sqlite/REPORT.md`, cited "SQLite §n"); the outline (cited "outline", decisions
B1–B17, questions E1–E14).

---

## 1. Scope and conformance

### 1.1 What the dialect is

The dialect is the contract between **front ends** that emit LLVM IR containing calls to
`__bennett_*` marker functions and the **plugin** that, at the final link of a whole program,
recognises those calls, analyses and normalises the code they reach, and maps it onto the ISA
(ISA §1.1). It fixes:

- the marker ABI, operand encodings and C header (§3);
- the processing order, execution tiers, polyvariant variants and function addresses (§4);
- the device analysis: points-to, value sets and every question answered from them (§5);
- the handle convention, provenance, comparison and collections of handles (§6);
- regions: kinds, candidate sets, dispatch, identity, well-formedness (§7);
- the oracle interface: the descriptor, parameter and device bindings, entry state, wide outputs
  and in-place updates (§8);
- budgets (§9);
- taint-driven lifting of classical code under control (§10);
- effects and device models per tier (§11);
- the materialised normalisation and the residual-poison validator (§12), floating point
  (§13), calls and control transfer (§14), inline asm and target intrinsics (§15);
- the lifting map from materialised LLVM to ISA objects (§16);
- the device classical memory model, CC and R lowering rules and fault outcomes (§17);
- a reference semantics from marker programs to the ISA formal model (§18);
- the driver's modes and plugin-invocation interface (§19);
- the platform profile `riscv64-bennett-virt` (§20);
- the legality corpus and conformance suites (§21); diagnostics (§22); versioning (§23);
  deferred mechanics (§24); blockers and ISA items (§25).

It does not fix the plugin's optimisation pipeline after normalisation, the plugin's device
objects beyond the constraints of §18.7, or any format the ISA owns.

### 1.2 Conformance classes

| Class | Who | Obligation |
|---|---|---|
| Producer | front ends (clang, rustc, Bennett.jl, importers) | emit modules that satisfy §3 and the outlining conventions of §7.6; never emit a reserved name of §3.1 other than a marker |
| Plugin | `bennett-plugin` and competitors | the processing order of §4.1; give every module the verdict the rules give it, with the diagnostics of §22; produce ISA objects whose execution equals the reference semantics (§18.7); suites DA–DX (§21.3) |
| Driver | `bennett-driver` | §19; links `bennett-runtimes` and `bennett-arith` bitcode of the link's LLVM major (§19.1); builds the comparison link and the native comparison binary (§17.7); emits blocker reports (§25) |
| Validator | the plugin's validator and `bennett-conformance` | the residual-poison validator (§12.4) and the reference recogniser (§3, §5, §6, §7), implemented independently; suites DA–DN |
| Runtime | `bennett-runtimes` and `bennett-arith` | define every entry point that §11, §13, §14, §17 and §20 name (prefixes `__bennett_rt_`, `__bennett_dev_`, `__bennett_sf_`, §3.1) with the semantics stated there, as ordinary bitcode compiled by the same pipeline; suites DR and DF |
| Platform | a platform profile of §20 | a board model plus shims satisfying §20; suite DP |

`scripts/dialect_check.py` in this repository is the seed of the pre-flight checker (§1.5). It
never bounds this document: an example it cannot yet execute fails the check (rule P-2, §1.5).

### 1.3 Relation to the ISA specification

The ISA is settled; this document never redefines it. Every ISA notion used here (tiers, wires,
`qreg` handles, oracles, actions, permutation actions, lprocs, F_B, budgets, tokens, fault
codes, the reference interpreter, interface and closure hashes, layouts, the launch ABI) has
exactly the meaning ISA §2–§17 give it. Where the dialect needs a construct ISA 0.8 lacks, this
document states (a) the full dialect mechanism, (b) its realisation on ISA 0.8 where one exists,
which may be a longer construction (an **efficiency item**, §25.3), and (c) otherwise a
**blocker** (§25.1) naming the exact ISA change. The dialect never shrinks to fit the ISA and
never extends the ISA unilaterally.

### 1.4 Departures from the north star

Only these remain. Every row of 0.1 §1.4 that narrowed scope (1.4-d, 1.4-e, 1.4-f) is deleted;
its replacement is §10, §9 and §8.7 respectively.

| North star | Dialect 0.2 | Why |
|---|---|---|
| §3.1 handle row: a handle through an `inttoptr`/`ptrtoint` round trip is rejected | Provenance flows through bitwise copies (§6.2) | Spike F1; maintainer decision 2; north-star amendment A2 (drafted with A1, bd `-25h`) |
| §3.1: regions entered as `__bennett_ctrl(q, @f, …)` with a constant `@f` | Region operands are classical data dispatched over a candidate set bounded by the program (§7.2) | Spike F3; maintainer decision 3 |
| Region named by symbol | Region identity is by content (§7.3) | Spike F2; maintainer decision 1 |
| §3.1 handle row is silent on comparison | `icmp eq/ne` of handles is identity (§6.3) | Maintainer decision 4 (2026-10-29; legality findings §3); a superset |
| §3.1, §4.2: a `qstore` into a register not known to be clean is a compile-time error | `qstore` is the ISA's XOR store; the compile-time rule is an optional warning `BD-W-QSTORE-UNCLEAN` (§6.7) | A register-granularity rule forbids the uncomputing second store; a superset of the north star (audit 1.4-g, D7) |
| §3.2: normalisation of "the outlined region" | Normalisation applies to every device variant (§12.1) | Device CC code is lowered to ISA CC, whose semantics differs from LLVM's poison semantics (ISA §4.1); a superset (audit 1.4-h) |

### 1.5 Notation and checked examples

LLVM IR is LLVM's textual form with opaque pointers, as accepted by LLVM 18, 20 and 23. `P(a)` is
the representation width of an integral pointer in address space a from DataLayout, falling
back to the default pointer entry (64 with no entry). `pw=P(0)` is the ISA address width,
not the width of every LLVM pointer. `I(a)` is its independently specified pointer-index width
(default P(a)). Numbers are decimal unless written `0x…`. A(F) is the function address of §4.4.

**Process rules** (also in `spec/README.md`):

- **P-1.** Every mechanism rule id (the steps S1–S9, rules N1–N35, V1–V16, TDL-1–TDL-12, the
  points-to rules PT1–PT12, the binding kinds of §8.2, the transforms of §14, the map rows of
  §16.4) has at least one checked `accept` example; every compile-time rejection of §22.1 has
  one `reject` example; every blocker of §25.1 has one `blocked` example. Appendix B is the
  index, and the checker derives it from the text: a rule id with no example fails the check.
- **P-2.** An example that the checker cannot yet execute **fails** the check with
  `checker-unimplemented: <capability>`. It is never skipped, and this document never removes
  or weakens an example to make the checker pass. The freeze gate is an all-green check.
- **P-3.** Every executed result comes from an independent executor: LLVM tools, natively built
  binaries, or the Rust ISA reference interpreter `bennett-isa-ref`. Python computes only
  *expected* artefacts (recogniser verdicts, normaliser output, map output, TDL events), and is
  never both producer and judge of one example.

Every fenced block whose info string starts with `llvm`, `isa`, `c`, `rust`, `julia`, `json` or
`text` followed by a tag below is a **checked example**. Its attributes are `key=value` pairs.
The last column names the checker capability (Appendix B) and, where the ISA reference
interpreter is the executor, the `bennett-isa-ref` command (`check`, `hash`, `run-oracle`,
`step-check` exist; `run-cc`, `reverse`, `run-action`, `run-program` are requested,
bd `b-isa-spec-xam`).

| Tag | Meaning; what the checker does | Capability / command |
|---|---|---|
| `llvm markers-ir` | the marker list of §3.2; compared with the C view | `parse`, `header` |
| `c markers-c` | the prototypes of the C header; byte-equal to `spec/bennett_markers.h` between its BEGIN/END PROTOTYPES lines | `header` |
| `llvm example=ID verdict=V` | a module; it parses and verifies on LLVM 18, 20 and 23 and the reference recogniser gives verdict `V` (`accept`, `reject:<code>` or `blocked:<code>`, §1.6). Optional attributes: `candidates=f,g` (the resolved candidate set of the first region operand, §7.2), `callees=f,g` (the candidate set of the first indirect call, §14.2), `shapes=N` (the number of compiled oracle shapes, §8.4), `mode=validate` (validator only, §12.4), `rmode=@f` (compiled in the whole-program R mode with entry f, §19.2) | `parse`, `recognise` |
| `llvm normalise-in=ID` / `llvm normalise-out=ID` | the normaliser (§12) applied to the first gives the second, lines compared after collapsing runs of blanks; both verify on the three majors (a source-only `majors=23` tag limits a form introduced in LLVM 23); the second passes the validator | `parse`, `normalise` |
| `text native=ID` | inputs and outputs of the native differential of a normalisation pair or a lifted example: both modules (or the C source and the module) built natively with the seeds of `spec/seeds/` (§21.4), run on the listed inputs, outputs equal to the listed values | `native` |
| `llvm lift=ID` | a materialised module; attribute `region=@f` names the region function and `desc=` the binding shape: the parameters' bindings in order (`IN(n,zext)`, `IN(n,sext)`, `ARG(W)`, `MEM_IN(A)`, `OUT(size)`), then `;dev=` device bindings (`ENTROPY(A)`, …) and `;tgt=` n(tgt) | `parse`, `map` |
| `text linkmap=ID` | the comparison-link facts the map of `lift=ID` reads (§19.5): `null_size`, `heap_base`, `heap_limit_max`, the globals with their bases, the A(F) of address-taken functions, and the budget table's largest `heap` and `stack` | `map` |
| `isa object=ID` | the reference object (§16.2) that the map produces from `lift=ID`, as complete ISA text; byte-equal to the map output; `bennett-isa-ref check` accepts it | `map`, `check` |
| `text hash=ID` | the interface hashes of the object's symbols (`bennett-isa-ref hash`) | `hash` |
| `text run=ID` | lines `input=… [mem=name:hex] [arg=…] budget=s,h,st,d -> code=C payload=P steps=N`; each line is the output of `bennett-isa-ref run-oracle <object> ref` with those options | `run-oracle` |
| `text step-check=ID` | a prefix-reversal claim; the output of `bennett-isa-ref step-check <object> ref --exhaustive` with the stated options | `step-check` |
| `llvm semantics=ID corpus=NAME` | a marker program; byte-equal to `corpus/legality/cases/NAME.ll`; run through the trace runtime and the reference semantics (§18) | `trace`, `run-action` |
| `text events=ID` / `text state=ID` | the event list (§18.2) of a marker program on an input, and the state vector before event `at` | `trace`, `run-action` |
| `text tdl=ID` | the TDL partition (§10, TDL-3) and generated event list of an action region invocation | `tdl`, `run-action` |
| `text cc-run=ID` | a CC or R run: results, fault records, tape, and the state after `bennett_reverse` | `run-cc`, `reverse` |
| `text blocker=ID` | the blocker report (§25.1) the plugin emits for a `blocked:` example | `recognise` |
| `json budget-table=ID` | a budget table (§9.2); parsed and resolved against the example it names | `driver` |
| `c example=ID`, `rust example=ID`, `julia example=ID` | a source file compiled by the toolchains of `corpus/legality/toolchains.toml` (`clang -O2 -flto` on 18, 20, 23, with `-I spec`; with `rmode=` against the `bennett-runtimes` musl sysroot, for which the checker's seeds stand in; `rustc --emit=llvm-bc`; Julia `code_llvm`) and then `lto<O3>`; the result is recognised with verdict `verdict=V` | `frontend`, `recognise` |

A module example that calls a marker or a primitive without declaring it implicitly contains
its declaration (§3.2, §3.1). **Section references**: `§n` is a section of this document, `ISA
§n` of the ISA specification, `NS §n` of `Bennett-NorthStar.md`; a reference that continues a
list (`ISA §4.7, §5.3`) names the same document as the reference before it. Fenced blocks
without a tag are illustrations and are non-normative; an `llvm` or `isa` block without a tag is
an error of this document.

The candidates attribute MUST continue to assert the resolved set of the first region operand in module instruction order, including nested-marker examples. [deps: DLT-1.5-P9, DLT-4.1, DLT-7.2, ISA-4.7]

### 1.6 Diagnostics and verdicts

A module's **verdict** is one of:

- `accept`: the plugin produces its objects;
- `reject:<code>`: a compile-time rejection of §22.1. The plugin MUST fail the compilation
  (nonzero exit, no output object) and report at least the first diagnostic in the processing
  order of §4.1;
- `blocked:<code>`: the module is accepted by the dialect, but at least one program point needs
  a capability that ISA 0.8 cannot express (§25.1). The plugin MUST produce its objects with
  every such point lowered to a **blocker trap** (§25.1: a run-time fault on exactly the
  executions that reach the point), MUST emit the blocker report of §25.1 and MUST exit with the
  distinct status 3. The code is the first blocker report in processing order.

The R-entry BLK-5 classification of §11.5 and §25.1 applies even when all supplied
   `shim_hash`, ISA §13);
3. in a CC variant only, a **host call** through `hostcall` (§11.4);
4. otherwise **trapped**: the call is replaced by F(8) (§12.3) and the coverage report has a
   `trapped` line for it (NS §3.3: "Calls with no bitcode are coverage-report lines (*trapped*),
   never silent").

An R or L variant never makes a host call (ISA §4.1: `hostcall` is CC only; NS §4.2: coherent
regions are closed). *(non-normative)* Item 4 is the only fault the dialect introduces for a
call, and it occurs only on the executions that reach the call; it is the north star's own rule.

## 5. The device analysis (one fixpoint)

The device analysis is a whole-program, flow-insensitive, inclusion-based (Andersen-style),
field-sensitive points-to and value-set analysis over every device variant and every host
function that exchanges data with one (passes it a pointer, or shares a global it accesses). It
is specified exactly: **every verdict, candidate set and dispatch domain of this document is
computed from the reference analysis below.** A plugin MAY use a more precise analysis to shrink
dispatch domains or arena placement in its lowering, never to change a verdict (the run-time
value is always a member of the reference set, so the results are unchanged).

### 5.1 Abstract objects and atoms

The **abstract objects** are:

- each `alloca` instruction;
- each global variable;
- each **allocation site**: a call of a function that carries `allockind("alloc")` or
  `allocsize(...)`, or whose name is one of `malloc`, `calloc`, `realloc`, `aligned_alloc`,
  `posix_memalign` (the object is the one written through its first argument), `memalign`,
  `strdup`, `strndup`, `_Znwm`, `_Znam`, `_ZnwmRKSt9nothrow_t`, `_ZnamRKSt9nothrow_t`,
  `_ZnwmSt11align_val_t`, `_ZnamSt11align_val_t`, `__rust_alloc`, `__rust_alloc_zeroed`,
  `__rust_realloc`, `julia.gc_alloc_obj`, `ijl_gc_alloc_typed`, `jl_gc_alloc`,
  `ijl_gc_small_alloc`, `ijl_gc_big_alloc`, or `__bennett_nz_alloc`;
- the pseudo-object `EXT` (memory of unknown provenance: host memory reached through a pointer
  the analysis did not create).

Heap objects are context-insensitive: one object per allocation-site instruction of the variant
in which it occurs. The list above is the reference list; `bennett-runtimes` declares its
allocator entry points with `allockind("alloc")` so that they are covered by the first rule.

An **offset** is an integer byte offset, a **residue class** (r mod s)[lo, hi) with s ≥ 2 (every
offset o with o ≡ r (mod s) and lo ≤ o < hi), or `*` (any offset). An **atom** is one of:

- `(X, o)`: a pointer into object X at offset o;
- `FN(F)`: the address A(F) of a defined function F;
- `ROOT(r)`: a handle with root r (the allocation site of a `qalloc`/`qborrow` call, §6.5);
- `INT(c)`: the integer constant c (of the value's width).

Every SSA value v (per lane for vectors, per index path for aggregates) has an **abstract
value** AV(v) = (A, t): a finite set A of atoms and a flag t; t = ⊤ means "and any other integer
or address". ⊤ never stands for a `ROOT` atom (handle provenance is always explicit); for a
function-pointer operand it stands for "any function of the operand's type whose address is
used" (§7.2, §14.2). Every slot (X, o) of memory has a content AV(X, o) of the same form. When A
holds more than **K = 4096** `INT` atoms they are dropped and t is set (a normative constant: it
decides dispatch and blocker verdicts, so §24 does not defer it). "AV(v) = ⊤" below means t is
set; unions and inclusions are taken component-wise.

Abstract-object and return propagation MUST cover attributed allocator calls and defined allocator wrappers through their underlying allocation sites without requiring the wrapper's name in a whitelist. [deps: DLT-5.1-P4, DLT-5.1-P6, DLT-5.2-P10, ISA-7.1, ISA-7.2]

### 5.2 Rules

Before the fixpoint, seed the initialiser atoms of every global, mutable or constant,
into its slots. For each alloca and allocation-site object, also represent every extent
whose initial contents are zero under §§17.1/17.3 as a zero-byte initial image; represent a
nonconstant extent symbolically over its possible allocated bytes. Subsequent stores union
additional possibilities and never erase these initial possibilities. This is an initial
image, not a claim that the object remains immutable.
The analysis is the least fixpoint of the following rules over AV. "Copies" means AV(result) ⊇
AV(operand) lane by lane; ⊤ absorbs. S2 models the value effects of the specified
normalisation before verdicts and dispatch domains freeze; pre-normalisation syntax does
not permit omitting a value produced by the materialised execution.

| Rule | Instruction or source | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| PT1 | `alloca`, global `@G`, allocation-site call | AV ∋ (X, 0); zero-filled initial extents are seeded as above |
| PT2 | `getelementptr T, ptr p, idx…` | uses P(a), I(a) and the complete finite-width computation of §16.4, also for constant expressions: sequential indices are sign-extended/truncated to I(a), scaling and offset accumulation wrap modulo 2^I(a), and addition preserves pointer bits above I(a). Zero accumulated offset copies AV(p). For every (X,o), include the image of all represented base addresses and index values (unknown base bits range over patterns consistent with DataLayout and X's alignment), using integer/residue-class/`*` offset unions. Unbounded mathematical addition is not a substitute. If an address may leave X, include (EXT, `*`) and the non-handle top flag; PT6 propagates this unknown-address alternative. The unknown-address alternative does not by itself establish host escape (§6.4): retain the original base provenance for that test; actual host/unknown origins keep their restrictions. ROOT provenance and §6.2 restrictions remain explicit. Array dimensions determine stride, never an index bound |
| PT3 | `phi`, `select` (value operands), `freeze`, `addrspacecast`; `bitcast` | the first four copy (union). A bitcast copies lane by lane only when source and destination have identical lane counts and per-lane representation widths, treating a scalar as one lane. Otherwise use the virtual N21 bitcast expansion of §12.6: pack source-lane bit patterns into its prescribed bit string in DataLayout order, then resegment at the destination representation width. Compute output AV through PT4/PT5 transfers of that expansion; finite INT lane sets denote their full Cartesian product, and K applies per resulting lane. Never copy a source-lane INT atom as the value of a packed destination scalar. Unknown bits propagate non-handle top through those transfers. Explicit object and ROOT provenance follows the same expansion and remains subject to §6.2; top never substitutes for ROOT. Apply also to constant expressions and fixed-vscale forms during S2 before verdicts, candidate sets, shapes and dispatch domains freeze. Direct finite bit-string evaluation is equivalent; no LLVM optimisation is required |
| PT4 | `ptrtoint`, `inttoptr`, `zext`/`sext`/`trunc` | copy only bit-preserving conversions. Otherwise apply the actual source/destination-width bit-vector conversion (§16.4): ptrtoint/inttoptr zero-extend or truncate at P(a), sext sign-extends. Use PT2’s represented-address image construction, retaining originating object and ROOT provenance. A narrowed representation that cannot be reassociated with its original object adds (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤; numerical uncertainty is not host escape. Apply also to constant expressions |
| PT5 | `add`/`sub` of a value with pointer atoms and an `INT` set | computes address bits modulo 2^W at the integer operation’s width W, never unbounded offset addition. Relative displacements from the originating base are modular too: zero net displacement represents that base again, including after an inttoptr truncation to P(a). Use PT2’s represented-address image construction at W bits (full-width arithmetic, no preserved high GEP bits), retaining original object and ROOT provenance. Keep modular displacement alternatives even outside the allocation, and additionally include (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤ when an image may leave it. Residue classes and `*` take the same finite-width image; an unrepresentable image retains (X, `*`) for each originating X plus (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤. Other integer operations on pointer atoms use that conservative image. Widening a pointer-derived integer by zext/sext uses that conservative image too (fixed reference fallback); ptrtoint zero-extension retains its base representation. Numerical uncertainty does not itself establish host escape (PT2). The rules apply also to constant expressions, before candidate/dispatch domains freeze; on finite `INT` sets, every LLVM integer operation, cast and `icmp` is evaluated elementwise; with x ⊤ and a constant m, `and x, m` gives the submasks of m and `urem x, m` gives {0, …, m−1} (each still subject to K); a range attribute or `!range` metadata removed by N3/N4 is not a bound on materialised execution and MUST NOT restrict any verdict, candidate set, descriptor shape or dispatch domain. Only bounds independently established by materialised instructions restrict the set; otherwise retain ⊤ and apply the ordinary operation rules, including finite masks/remainders |
| PT6 | `load T, ptr p` | for each (X,o) ∈ AV(p): memory atoms carry their storage interval and representation width; copy a whole-value atom only for that identical interval with a compatible representation. A known immutable byte image decodes exactly the accessed bytes using DataLayout and T (including initialisers). For a partial, spanning or overlapping access whose result bits are not represented exactly, the non-handle value is ⊤, never the union of component INT values. X=EXT gives ⊤. A non-handle top flag on p contributes the same unknown-address load alternative even when explicit object offsets overlap no known slot; it MUST NOT be dropped. ROOT provenance remains explicit and §6.2's partial-handle restrictions still apply. For a possibly valid load wholly contained in a zero-filled initial extent, include the all-zero value of the load’s representation and width, unioned with values from every overlapping store or copy under these interval rules. PT8 propagates these initial byte possibilities when copying memory. Zero contributes no ROOT. Region inputs, replayed caller bytes and other nonzero-initialised contents retain their specified sources; this rule does not replace them with zeros |
| PT7 | `store v, ptr p` | retain each written interval and representation width; partial writes overlapping a larger load participate in PT6's conservative fallback. AV(X, o + d_k) ⊇ AV(v_k) for each (X, o) ∈ AV(p) and each lane or field k at byte offset d_k |
| PT8 | memory copy (`llvm.memcpy`/`memmove`/`memcpy.inline`, `__bennett_rt_memmove`) of (dst, src, len); memory fill (`llvm.memset`, `__bennett_rt_memset`) of (dst, v, len) | copy: propagate zero-byte initial images over the copied byte ranges, including their symbolic extents; for each slot (Y, s) of each (Y, o_s) ∈ AV(src) and (Z, o_d) ∈ AV(dst): when offsets and len are constants and the atom's entire storage interval [s, s + size(atom)) lies in the copied range, copy its atom and representation width to (Z, o_d + s − o_s). Every partially overlapping atom instead contributes non-handle ⊤ and explicit ROOT provenance to the affected destination interval (unknown offsets/len use `*`); do not copy the whole component INT. Fill: every slot in [o_d, o_d + len) of each (Z, o_d) ∈ AV(dst) (all of Z when an offset or len is not constant) gets the `INT` atoms of the byte v repeated to the slot's width (⊤ when v is not constant) |
| PT9 | direct call of G, or indirect call with candidate G (§14.2) | AV(parameter i of G) ⊇ AV(argument i); AV(result) ⊇ the union of AV over G's `ret` operands; `byval` arguments copy the pointee's slots into the callee's copy object |
| PT10 | `insertelement`/`extractelement`/`shufflevector`/`insertvalue`/`extractvalue` | extraction/insertion use N15’s total unsigned index semantics. Constant in-range extraction copies its lane; constant out-of-range extraction gives the element type’s zero. Dynamic extraction unions every source lane selected by a represented index, plus zero iff the index set includes an unsigned value >= L (including top when 2^K > L). No zero is added if every represented index is in range; zero adds no ROOT. In-range insertion replaces the selected lane; out-of-range insertion preserves the vector. Dynamic insertion includes each old lane unless it is certainly replaced, and the inserted value whenever that lane can be selected. Valid shuffle indices and aggregate paths move atoms unchanged (handle-table reads, audit B-23) |
| PT11 | a parameter of a function with a host caller, a device entry, or a function whose address host code uses | AV ⊇ {(EXT, `*`)} for `ptr` parameters, ⊤ for integer parameters, plus the atoms of the device-side arguments by PT9; for a pointer parameter i of a device entry E, also the candidate atom ROOT(E, i) of §6.6 |
| PT12 | a `qalloc`/`qborrow` call; a call of a function with no definition that resolves to a host call (§4.5) or is trapped; an `atomicrmw`/`cmpxchg` (before N9) | `qalloc`/`qborrow`: AV ∋ ROOT(site) (§6.5). An undefined callee: the result is ⊤ and every slot reachable from its pointer arguments gets ⊤. Before domains freeze, PT12 MUST apply PT6 to the old value, the PT5 transfers of the full N9 update, and PT7 to the actual stored value, with cmpxchg returning its old-value and success fields and storing the selected new-or-old value. [deps: DLT-5.2-P1, DLT-5.2-P6, DLT-5.2-P7, DLT-5.2-P8, DLT-5.2-P13, DLT-12.2-P11, ISA-4.1, ISA-4.2] |

An `inttoptr` of a constant gives (EXT, `*`) and, for function-pointer purposes, ⊤: function
addresses are symbolic `FN` atoms during the analysis, and their numeric values A(F) are fixed
only by the comparison link (§19.5), after normalisation (S7) and before lifting (S8).

```

```text state=pt6-byte-width
{"value_set":[257],"width":257,"wire":256,"live_after":0,"trap":0}
```

```llvm example=pt6-subrange verdict=blocked:BD-B-ISA4

define void @top() {
  %p = alloca i32
  store i32 66049, ptr %p
  %w = load i16, ptr %p, align 1
  %n = zext i16 %w to i64
  %h = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 %n)
  call void @__bennett_qfree(ptr %h)
  ret void
}
```

```text blocker=pt6-subrange
BD-B-ISA4 %n: partial mutable load has TOP width; BLK-1 (ISA-4)
```

```text state=pt6-subrange
{"value_set":"TOP","width":513,"live_after":0,"trap":0}
```

A constant-byte fill carries its complete filled interval and byte value; a load
contained in that interval decodes the repeated byte at the load's width. Other
overlapping writes still participate in PT6 (a fill does not erase them).
PT8 copies preserve storage intervals/representation widths only for fully copied
atoms. Partial copies and fills add overlapping affected intervals; PT6 then returns
⊤ for any load whose whole bits are not represented exactly. These are fixed
reference rules, not optional precision improvements.

Handle-array propagation MUST retain field intervals and finite-width address images so that a disjoint integer counter is not handle-derived, without treating an array type as a proof that indices stay in bounds. [deps: DLT-5.1-P7, DLT-5.2-P3, DLT-5.2-P7, DLT-5.2-P8, ISA-4.1, ISA-4.2]

**Compatible allocation/collection witness (non-normative).** A defined wrapper
returns an attributed fresh allocation whose name is not a built-in allocator.
A two-level heap header carries a handle through a shared global; a disjoint
counter remains ordinary integer data. Two indirect candidates return different
ROOTs. An i1 vector index selects exactly one live lane without an out-of-range
zero alternative. Both inputs execute four X gates, two clean frees and code0;
LLVM18/23 source and materialised O0/O3 execute independently of the analysis.

```llvm example=compatible-allocator-graph verdict=accept callees=first,second
@shared = internal global { ptr, i64 } zeroinitializer
@observed_old = global i64 0
@observed_success = global i64 0
declare ptr @custom_alloc(i64) allocsize(0)
declare void @free(ptr)
define internal ptr @make_node(ptr %h) {
  %node = call ptr @custom_alloc(i64 16)
  store ptr %h, ptr %node
  %link = getelementptr i8, ptr %node, i64 8
  store ptr null, ptr %link
  ret ptr %node
}
define internal ptr @first(ptr %a, ptr %b) {
  ret ptr %a
}
define internal ptr @second(ptr %a, ptr %b) {
  ret ptr %b
}
define void @host_count() {
  %counter = getelementptr { ptr, i64 }, ptr @shared, i64 0, i32 1
  store i64 9, ptr %counter
  ret void
}
define void @top(i1 %choose) {
  %a = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 1)
  %b = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 1)
  %node = call ptr @make_node(ptr %a)
  %header = call ptr @custom_alloc(i64 8)
  store ptr %node, ptr %header
  store ptr %header, ptr @shared
  %loaded_header = load ptr, ptr @shared
  %loaded_node = load ptr, ptr %loaded_header
  %from_node = load ptr, ptr %loaded_node
  %f = select i1 %choose, ptr @first, ptr @second
  %from_callback = call ptr %f(ptr %from_node, ptr %b)
  call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %from_callback, i64 0)
  call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %from_callback, i64 0)
  %v0 = insertelement <2 x ptr> zeroinitializer, ptr %a, i32 0
  %v1 = insertelement <2 x ptr> %v0, ptr %b, i32 1
  %lane = extractelement <2 x ptr> %v1, i1 %choose
  call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %lane, i64 0)
  call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %lane, i64 0)
  call void @host_count()
  %counter = getelementptr { ptr, i64 }, ptr @shared, i64 0, i32 1
  %count = load i64, ptr %counter
  store i64 %count, ptr @observed_old
  call void @free(ptr %node)
  call void @free(ptr %header)
  call void @__bennett_qfree(ptr %a)
  call void @__bennett_qfree(ptr %b)
  ret void
}
```

```text state=compatible-allocator-graph at=return
{"roots":{"%from_node":["top%a"],"%from_callback":["top%a","top%b"],"%lane":["top%a","top%b"]},"counter_roots":[],"native":[[9,2,4,2,0,0],[9,2,4,2,0,0]]}
```

### 5.3 Value sets

The **value set** VS(v) of an integer or pointer operand is the set of its `INT` and `FN` atoms
(pointer atoms denote addresses that are not compile-time constants and make VS(v) = ⊤ for
dispatch purposes), or ⊤. A value set is a precision tool only:

- an operand with a finite VS is dispatched over it (§3.4, §14.2);
- an operand with VS = ⊤ is dispatched over its type's whole valid domain where that domain is
  finite: operation codes and classes (§3.4), binding kinds (§8.4), the function addresses of a
  type (§7.2, §14.2);
- otherwise it is a blocker case of §25.1: widths (BLK-1), budgets computed on the device
  (BLK-2), parameters of in-place pairs (§8.7).

### 5.4 Uses

| Result | Read by |
|---|---|
| `FN` atoms of region operands | region candidate sets (§7.2) |
| `FN` atoms of indirect callees | indirect-call candidate sets (§14.2) |
| `ROOT` atoms | handle provenance (§6.2), handle containment (§6.4), static register files (§6.5) |
| `INT` atoms of descriptor and budget fields | descriptor shapes (§8.4), budget pre-minting (§9.5) |
| slots stored under control | the may-Co analysis and arena placement (§10, TDL-2, TDL-4) |
| objects loaded by L code | the oracle read set R and entry-state transfer (§8.5) |
| `INT` atoms of `op`, `cls`, `n` | dispatch domains (§3.4) |

```llvm example=pt-variable-index verdict=accept
%struct.tab = type { [4 x ptr], i64 }

define void @t(i64 %i) {
  %s = alloca %struct.tab
  %h0 = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 1)
  store ptr %h0, ptr %s
  %pn = getelementptr %struct.tab, ptr %s, i64 0, i32 1
  store i64 3, ptr %pn
  %k = and i64 %i, 3
  %pk = getelementptr %struct.tab, ptr %s, i64 0, i32 0, i64 %k
  %hk = load ptr, ptr %pk
  call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %hk, i64 0)
  %n = load i64, ptr %pn
  %n2 = add i64 %n, 1
  store i64 %n2, ptr %pn
  call void @__bennett_qfree(ptr %h0)
  ret void
}
```

*(non-normative)* In `pt-variable-index`, `%pk` has the offset class (0 mod 8)[0, 32) in `%s`
(the `[4 x ptr]` array), so the load `%hk` reads the slots 0, 8, 16, 24: AV(`%hk`) =
{ROOT(`%h0`)}. The count field is at the exact offset 32, outside the class, so AV(`%n`) holds
INT(3) and, through the store of `%n2`, INT(4), INT(5), … until it exceeds K and becomes ⊤; it
holds no `ROOT` atom, and the arithmetic on it is legal (audit B-27: 0.1 gave the load offset `*`
and rejected this program).

**Finite-width and materialised-domain witnesses (round 6).** Under I=32,
2^32 truncates to zero, so the table load yields FN(body) and dispatch succeeds.
For the ranged inputs, N3/N4 erase the annotations: `n & 3` gives {0,1,2,3}
and `| 1` gives {1,3}. At n=2 width 3 admits both X gates on wire 2 and a clean
free. No new input precondition or fault is introduced.

```llvm example=pt2-wrap verdict=accept

declare void @__bennett_adj(ptr, ptr, ptr)

 ret void
}

 %p = getelementptr i8, ptr @tab, i64 4294967296
 %f = load ptr, ptr %p, align 8
 call void @__bennett_adj(ptr %f, ptr null, ptr null)
 ret void
}
```
