## 6. Handles (handles 0.2.8)

### 6.1 Representation


 succeeded in 231ms:
     1	# 5u3 — exact pointer fixpoints without verdict-changing caps
     2	
     3	Proposal for the dialect council; `b-dialect-spec-5u3`, linked to
     4	`bennett-plugin` bp-fj1. This job writes no dialect or plugin source.
     5	
     6	## Defect classification and North Star scope
     7	
     8	Both dialect `Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md` (draft 0.2.12) and the 0.3 draft
     9	§5 require every verdict/candidate/dispatch domain to come from the
    10	specified reference analysis. §5.1 caps INT atoms at K=4096, but does not
    11	cap explicit object offsets or PT6–PT8 memory intervals. PT2/PT5 use
    12	finite-width modular pointer arithmetic. Hence an explicit enumeration
    13	is finite but can have 2^61 members. §5.1 already admits residue classes
    14	and `*`; it does not require enumerative storage.
    15	
    16	The bead establishes a severe implementation/performance problem, **not
    17	by itself an inconsistent or undefined reference semantics**. A sound
    18	over-approximation may still change a compile-time verdict, which §5
    19	forbids. The proposed thresholds (4096 offsets, 4 outside extent, 32 for
    20	unknown extent; max(64,extent/4) slots) are not justified as exact. They
    21	must not become normative merely because one plugin uses them.
    22	
    23	North Star §§0/3.3/6.1 require arbitrary defined bounded programs,
    24	whole-program analysis and substitutable compilers; §8 rung 4 requires
    25	unmodified SQLite. Widening that introduces a false handle-use/escape
    26	rejection narrows those capabilities. Removing SQLite features, changing
    27	its source, suppressing vectorisation or declaring valid pointer patterns
    28	unsupported does not resolve the issue. Keep bp-fj1 open for practical
    29	convergence; do not declare the rung delivered from a coarse fixpoint.
    30	
    31	## Plugin evidence actually inspected
    32	
    33	`../bennett-plugin-rung4/.tmp/rung4/PROGRESS.md` reports 177381 slots
    34	plateauing by round 7 on the N=16, LLVM-18 closure, with additional atoms
    35	still growing. The tail of `.tmp/rung4/prof/trace-k.log` shows round 39,
    36	177381 slots, growth in SQLite integer/value sets, then
    37	`§5.2 PT8/PT9/PT12 budget exhausted` at a memcpy in
    38	`@sqlite3CreateForeignKey`, exit 1 after 3401.31 seconds. It does **not**
    39	demonstrate a converged exact reference fixpoint or prove that remaining
    40	growth is only a pointer chain. The supplied earlier 800k-slot report is
    41	historical bead evidence, not reproduced here. No full sibling gate was
    42	run, and no plugin implementation was copied.
    43	
    44	## Alternatives and worked recurrence
    45	
    46	Take width W=64 and an unfiltered inclusion cycle with one provenance X:
    47	
    48	```text
    49	S initially contains offset 0
    50	S includes { (o+8) mod 2^64 : o in S }
    51	```
    52	
    53	Its exact least solution is `{o: o=0 mod 8, 0<=o<2^64}`. There are
    54	`2^64/gcd(8,2^64)=2^61` offsets, representable by one residue class.
    55	For W=8 the set is exactly 0,8,...,248 (32 offsets), followed by 0.
    56	Starting at 3 instead gives 3,11,...,251; adding a seed at 4 gives two
    57	cosets, not `*`. Extent=16 does not permit dropping offsets ≥16:
    58	PT2 retains original provenance and adds EXT/top for possibly invalid
    59	addresses; it does not turn numerical out-of-range into host escape.
    60	
    61	A full-width same-object memory copy from offset o to o+8 can similarly
    62	propagate one stored atom along that relation. An exact periodic memory
    63	summary must retain storage width, intervals and value/root provenance;
    64	merging all fields into one slot may turn a nearby integer field into a
    65	handle-derived value. That would be a different verdict even though it
    66	is conservative for aliasing.
    67	
    68	| Alternative | Assessment |
    69	|---|---|
    70	| Unbounded explicit enumeration | Same reference semantics, often impractical. Not required by the existing text. |
    71	| Normative widening with the bead's thresholds | Deterministic only with further rules for ordering/stickiness/joins; can reject previously admitted programs. Not acceptable without a capability-preserving lowering for every newly coarse case. |
    72	| Exact symbolic sets and translation-cycle closure (recommended) | Preserves verdicts and handles this witness compactly. Does not claim every SQLite recurrence has this form. |
    73	| Resource/time cap with an explicit unfinished build | Honest operational outcome, not a completed analysis or a source rejection. Remains implementation work. |
    74	
    75	## Recommended exact spec diff
    76	
    77	Add a dialect K/clarification adjacent to §5.1's K and §5.2's least
    78	fixpoint, in both maintained maps:
    79	
    80	```text
    81	The reference bounds INT atoms as above; it imposes no additional
    82	cardinality bound on pointer-offset sets or memory intervals. Implementers
    83	MAY use exact symbolic sets, shared interval relations and SCC
    84	acceleration instead of enumerating represented offsets or slots.
    85	All reference sets and byte/representation-width distinctions must be
    86	preserved for verdicts, candidate sets and dispatch domains. A coarser
    87	summary is not the reference result merely because it is sound for
    88	aliasing. An unfinished analysis reports an unfinished build with the
    89	rule and exhausted implementation resource, not a source rejection,
    90	completed verdict or run-time fault for a valid input.
    91	
    92	In a pure modular-translation inclusion SCC, if every cycle transfer is
    93	an unfiltered fixed displacement at one bit width W and preserves one
    94	base/provenance coordinate, close the generated displacements as a
    95	subgroup of Z/(2^W). For a single self-cycle of stride s, each seed a
    96	denotes the coset a modulo gcd(s,2^W). Union distinct seed cosets. For
    97	multiple vertices, retain vertex-specific path displacements; do not
    98	merge their seed coordinates. Apply the same rule to a memory relation
    99	only when every transfer preserves the complete storage interval and
   100	representation width and all PT6–PT8 read/overlap results are identical
   101	to the represented individual slots. Retain every ROOT and FN alternative
   102	explicitly. Otherwise use the original rules or another exact method.
   103	PT2's pointer-index width and preserved high pointer bits still apply;
   104	this rule does not replace them by unrestricted full-pointer addition.
   105	```
   106	
   107	Add to §24:
   108	
   109	```text
   110	Analysis representation/acceleration is implementation policy; the
   111	default result is the exact §5 least fixpoint, including existing K=4096
   112	INT abstraction. Time or memory caps do not alter that result. A report
   113	of unfinished analysis keeps the remaining implementation obligation open.
   114	```
   115	
   116	This recommends an exact acceleration and explicitly **declines new
   117	verdict-affecting bounds**. If council instead wants a new abstract
   118	domain, it must supply canonical joins, evaluation order independence,
   119	ROOT/partial-access rules and a lowering that preserves all programs
   120	previously in scope; the current bead does not establish those properties.
   121	
   122	## Hashes, catalog and consumers
   123	
   124	An exact representation clarification changes no materialised program,
   125	ISA object/certificate or catalog bytes. A dialect publication still
   126	gets its required revision/mapping identity. A threshold widening could
   127	change descriptors, dispatch domains, diagnostics and emitted object
   128	hashes, so cannot masquerade as a local plugin optimization. Plugin and
   129	independent dialect checker should compare normalized exact results on
   130	small-width cases, not implementation data-structure counts.
   131	
   132	## Conformance vectors and verification
   133	
   134	Enumerate all W=8, stride-8 cases above and compare their symbolic cosets;
   135	W=64 is a symbolic cardinality test, never a 2^61-element allocation.
   136	Test stride 0, stride 1, multiple seeds, a cycle with a filter (must not
   137	use the pure-cycle rule), and GEP with I(a)<P(a). Memory vectors include
   138	same-width full copies, overlapping partial copies and adjacent handle/
   139	integer fields; retain PT6's non-handle top and ROOT distinctions. Check
   140	identical verdicts independent of worklist order. Mathematical cosets are
   141	checked by [contract-verification](2026-11-02-contract-verification.md).
   142	The SQLite exact fixpoint and production gate remain unverified here.
   143	
   144	## Release and after acceptance
   145	
   146	Recommend a **dialect K clarification on the current 0.2/0.3 tracks**,
   147	not an ISA-0.10 dependency. As stated, 5u3 is primarily an implementation
   148	scalability request. After acceptance, add the finite vectors and let the
   149	plugin owner establish exact convergence on both LLVM majors and the
   150	full rung-4 closure. No new source restriction is authorized by this note.
# Contract derivations — review index and reproducible verification

These are proposals for council grading, not published spec changes.
Contract beads remain open for acceptance/implementation; b5b's owner
handles its implementation status. No git command was run by this job.

A recommendation for a 0.9 K repair means the existing council-reviewed
repair track, not permission to publish an incompatible change silently.
`spec/README.md` normally requires a full-version bump for semantic
changes. Council must settle that publication choice. Every statement
below that existing source hashes stay fixed assumes an authorized
in-version repair with unchanged envelope bytes; a full-version bump
instead changes all symbol/interface/closure envelopes and their consumer
pins. Catalog bytes/hash change only when that separately versioned
catalog is actually changed. No note here performs either publication.

## Exact arithmetic and document check

Run this extraction from the repository root under the environment prefix:

```sh
python3 -I -B - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
p = Path('docs/notes/2026-11-02-contract-verification.md')
source = p.read_text().split('```python\n', 1)[1].split('\n```', 1)[0]
exec(compile(source, str(p), 'exec'))
PY
```

The arithmetic is in Q[z]/(z^4+1), z=exp(i*pi/4), with rational
coefficients; H uses (z−z³)/2. There are no floating-point quantum
calculations in this check. Equality of every branch operator to V/sqrt(2)
establishes the resulting channel on all matrix units, hence also with
an external reference; the negative vectors compare density matrices.
This is exact finite mathematical verification, not a producer certificate
accepted by an implementation of the proposed contract.

```python
from fractions import Fraction as F
from pathlib import Path
from math import gcd
import hashlib
import re

def scalar(n):
    return (F(n), F(0), F(0), F(0))

ZERO, ONE = scalar(0), scalar(1)
Z = (F(0), F(1), F(0), F(0))

def add(a, b):
    return tuple(x + y for x, y in zip(a, b))

def neg(a):
    return tuple(-x for x in a)

def mul(a, b):
    out = [F(0)] * 7
    for i in range(4):
        for j in range(4):
            out[i+j] += a[i] * b[j]
    for k in range(6, 3, -1):
        out[k-4] -= out[k]
    return tuple(out[:4])

def conj(a):
    out = [a[0], F(0), F(0), F(0)]
    for k in range(1, 4):
        out[4-k] -= a[k]
    return tuple(out)

I = mul(Z, Z)
TINV = conj(Z)
HFACTOR = (F(0), F(1, 2), F(0), F(-1, 2))
assert mul(HFACTOR, HFACTOR) == scalar(F(1, 2))
assert mul(Z, TINV) == ONE
assert mul(I, I) == neg(ONE)

def compact(state):
    return {i: a for i, a in state.items() if a != ZERO}

def accumulate(state, i, a):
    state[i] = add(state.get(i, ZERO), a)

def enabled(i, controls):
    return all((i >> c) & 1 for c in controls)

def hadamard(state, wire, controls=()):
    out = {}
    mask = 1 << wire
    for i, a in state.items():
        if not enabled(i, controls):
            accumulate(out, i, a)
            continue
        a = mul(a, HFACTOR)
        low = i & ~mask
        accumulate(out, low, a)
        accumulate(out, low | mask, neg(a) if i & mask else a)
    return compact(out)

def flip(state, target, controls=()):
    out = {}
    for i, a in state.items():
        j = i ^ (1 << target) if enabled(i, controls) else i
        accumulate(out, j, a)
    return compact(out)

def phase(state, target, factor, controls=()):
    return compact({i: mul(a, factor) if (i >> target) & 1
                    and enabled(i, controls) else a
                    for i, a in state.items()})

# Bit positions: a=0, b=1, g=2, y=3, outer q=4.
C = [('h', 2), ('t', 2), ('cx', (0, 2)), ('tdg', 2),
     ('cx', (1, 2)), ('t', 2), ('cx', (0, 2)),
     ('tdg', 2), ('h', 2), ('sdg', 2)]
adj = {'h': 'h', 'cx': 'cx', 't': 'tdg', 'tdg': 't',
       's': 'sdg', 'sdg': 's'}
CDAG = [(adj[op], args) for op, args in reversed(C)]

def sequence(state, gates, controls=()):
    for op, args in gates:
        if op == 'h':
            state = hadamard(state, args, controls)
        elif op == 'cx':
            state = flip(state, args[1], (args[0],) + controls)
        else:
            factor = {'t': Z, 'tdg': TINV, 's': I, 'sdg': neg(I)}[op]
            state = phase(state, args, factor, controls)
    return state

for a in range(2):
    for b in range(2):
        i = a | (b << 1)
        assert sequence({i: ONE}, C) == {i | ((a*b) << 2): ONE}
        dirty_phase = I if (a, b) == (0, 1) else neg(I)
        assert sequence({i | 4: ONE}, C) == {
            i | ((1 ^ (a*b)) << 2): dirty_phase}

def expected(state, controlled, closed):
    outer = (4,) if controlled else ()
    state = flip(state, 3, (0, 1) + outer)
    return hadamard(state, 0, outer) if closed else state

def branches(state, controlled=False, closed=False, correction='correct'):
    outer = (4,) if controlled else ()
    state = sequence(state, C, outer)
    state = flip(state, 3, (2,) + outer)
    if closed:
        state = hadamard(state, 0, outer)
    state = hadamard(state, 2)  # Unconditional X-basis measurement.
    result = []
    for m in range(2):
        branch = {i: a for i, a in state.items() if ((i >> 2) & 1) == m}
        if m:
            branch = flip(branch, 2)  # Reset measured private wire.
            transport = closed and correction != 'stale'
            if transport:
                branch = hadamard(branch, 0, outer)
            if correction != 'none':
                controls = (0, 1) + (() if correction == 'uncontrolled' else outer)
                branch = {i: neg(a) if enabled(i, controls) else a
                          for i, a in branch.items()}
            if transport:
                branch = hadamard(branch, 0, outer)
        result.append(compact(branch))
    return result

branch_columns = 0
for controlled in (False, True):
    outer = (4,) if controlled else ()
    for i in range(32):
        if i & 4:
            continue
        start = {i: ONE}
        unitary = sequence(start, C, outer)
        unitary = flip(unitary, 3, (2,) + outer)
        unitary = sequence(unitary, CDAG, outer)
        assert unitary == expected(start, controlled, False)
        for closed in (False, True):
            want = {j: mul(a, HFACTOR)
                    for j, a in expected(start, controlled, closed).items()}
            for branch in branches(start, controlled, closed):
                assert branch == compact(want)
                branch_columns += 1
assert branch_columns == 128

def density(states):
    out = {}
    for state in states:
        for i, a in state.items():
            for j, b in state.items():
                accumulate(out, (i, j), mul(a, conj(b)))
    return compact(out)

superposition = {0: ONE}
for wire in (0, 1, 4):
    superposition = hadamard(superposition, wire)
for controlled, closed, correction in [
        (False, False, 'none'), (True, False, 'uncontrolled'),
        (False, True, 'stale')]:
    actual = density(branches(superposition, controlled, closed, correction))
    ideal = density([expected(superposition, controlled, closed)])
    assert actual != ideal, correction

# Imported model, unit conversion, sequence/parallel accounting.
def model(d):
    return 3*2*d, 2*d*d, F(1, 10) * F(1, 100)**((d+1)//2)
assert model(5) == (30, 50, F(1, 10**7))
assert model(3) == (18, 18, F(1, 100000))
assert F(30, 10**9) == F(3, 10**8)
assert 3*30*50 == 4500
assert (3*30, 50) == (90, 50)  # Sequential time/peak.
assert (30, 3*50) == (30, 150)  # Parallel time/peak.

# Width dispatch, persistent versus reversed load state.
assert (1 ^ 0, 5 ^ 3) == (1, 6)
def later_run(reverse):
    memory, bump = 7, 17  # A stored 7 and allocated at 16.
    if reverse:
        memory, bump = 0, 16
    return memory, bump
assert later_run(False) == (7, 17)
assert later_run(True) == (0, 16)

# Fixed-image coverage; hashing raw bytes differs from hashing hex text.
image_hash = hashlib.sha256(bytes([1, 2])).hexdigest()
assert image_hash != hashlib.sha256(b'0102').hexdigest()
assert 2**(1+1+4) == 64
assert F(64, 2**22) == F(1, 65536)
assert (2**16)*8 == 524288

# Exact modular orbit; no enormous enumeration.
for seed in (0, 3, 4):
    visited, current = set(), seed
    while current not in visited:
        visited.add(current)
        current = (current + 8) % 256
    assert visited == {x for x in range(256) if (x-seed) % 8 == 0}
    assert len(visited) == 32
assert (2**64)//gcd(8, 2**64) == 2**61

# Controlled X and -X produce orthogonal Bell states.
bell_plus = {0: HFACTOR, 3: HFACTOR}
bell_minus = {0: HFACTOR, 3: neg(HFACTOR)}
inner = ZERO
for i, a in bell_plus.items():
    inner = add(inner, mul(conj(a), bell_minus[i]))
assert inner == ZERO

# Fault counts concern code(F_B), not the incoming XOR word.
assert 4*4*16 == 256
assert 1 ^ 1 == 0  # Code 1 failure can leave final fault word zero.
assert 1 ^ 0 == 1  # Successful invocation can leave it nonzero.
assert (1+1, 2*2, max(1, 2)) == (2, 4, 2)  # Root time/occupancy/peak.
assert 1+2 != 2  # Child peaks cannot be summed to get root peak.

# Owned deliverable structure and local links.
base = Path('docs/notes')
ids = ['z4c', 'l2p', 'fd0', 'lpe', 'adg', 'xv4', 'b5b', '5u3',
       'checker-native-model', 'checker-diagnostics', 'checker-fault-rate',
       'checker-resource-contexts']
for ident in ids:
    path = base / ('2026-11-02-contract-' + ident + '.md')
    body = path.read_text()
    for word in ['Defect', 'North Star', 'Alternatives',
                 'Recommended exact spec diff', 'Hashes',
                 'Conformance', 'Release']:
        assert word.lower() in body.lower(), (ident, word)
    assert not re.search(r'\b(?:TODO|TBD)\b', body)
    assert sum(line.startswith('```') for line in body.splitlines()) % 2 == 0
    for target in re.findall(r'\[[^\]]+\]\(([^)]+)\)', body):
        if not target.startswith(('https://', 'http://', '#')):
            assert (path.parent / target.split('#')[0]).exists(), (ident, target)
print('PASS: 12 proposals; exact AND 8 columns/32 inverse columns; '
      '128 corrected branch columns; 3 negative channels; '
      'model/domain/orbit/resource arithmetic; fences and local links')
print('Fixed-image SHA-256:', image_hash)
```

## Verification limits

The exact checks establish the finite witnesses and internal document
consistency. They do not prove the proposed general contract sound in
all cases, accept a new K entry, close an implementation bead, or show
that SQLite's full reference analysis converges in practical time.
Each item states its own remaining acceptance vectors and consumer work.

 succeeded in 638ms:
## 22. Diagnostics

### 22.1 Compile-time rejections

Every row cites the north-star clause or maintainer decision that requires the rejection; a
rejection without one may not exist (standing rule).

| Code | Rule | Required by |
|---|---|---|
| `BD-E-VERSION` | §19.8, §19.9 | NS §3.2 (per-major builds, "one target datalayout", "tested compatibility envelope"); a tool mismatch, not a program property |
| `BD-E-MARKER-DEFINED` | §3.1 rule 1 | NS §3.1 ("declared external marker functions") |
| `BD-E-MARKER-UNKNOWN` | §3.1 rule 2 | NS §3.1 (recognition by name; reserved names) |
| `BD-E-MARKER-ADDRESS` | §3.1 rule 3 | NS §3.1 ("recognises them by name and signature": a marker is never called indirectly) |
| `BD-E-MARKER-SIGNATURE` | §3.5 | NS §3.1 ("fixed signatures") |
| `BD-E-MARKER-ATTRIBUTE` | §3.6 | NS §3.1 (the positive legality requirement) |
| `BD-E-JULIA-CCALL` | §3.8 | NS §3.3 (per-front-end integration; the `llvmcall` route keeps the capability, audit B-13) |
| `BD-E-EFFECT-LIE` | §3.8 | NS §3.1 (positive legality), NS §3.2 (definedness) |
| `BD-E-OPERAND` | §3.4 (a constant outside its table) | NS §3.1 (fixed operand encodings); the marker faults on every execution that reaches it |
| `BD-E-HANDLE-USE` | §6.2 | maintainer decision 2; NS §3.1 ("never dereferenced") |
| `BD-E-HANDLE-ESCAPE` | §6.4 | NS §3.1 ("any handle use that survives into host native code is rejected") |
| `BD-E-HANDLE-UNTRACKED` | §6.2 | NS §3.1 (handles are the results of `qalloc`; linearity is a fail-loud verifier obligation) |
| `BD-E-REGION-TYPE` | §7.2 (the unique candidate) | decision 3 (candidates of the required type); the call is undefined on every execution |
| `BD-E-REGION-MEASURE` | §7.5(a) (the unique candidate), unless deferred | NS §3.1 ("user measurement inside it is refused") |
| `BD-E-REVERSIBLE-MEASURE` | §7.5(b) | NS §4.2 (reversible tier); ISA §10 (a run that measured is not reversible) |
| `BD-E-RESIDUAL` | §12.4 | NS §3.2 (residual-poison validator) |
| `BD-E-INPLACE-NOT-INVERSE` | §8.7 | NS §3.1 ("an in-place update with no inverse fails loud at compile time") |
| `BD-E-COHERENT-OVERWRITE` | TDL-5(d) | NS §4.2 ("writing a register not known to be clean is a compile-time error … else it fails loud") |
| `BD-E-COHERENT-ANGLE` | TDL-10 | NS §4.2 (predication, not superposed parameters); ISA §3.1 ("angles are literals or classical arguments") |

**Deleted** from 0.1 (each was a narrowing, audit §1): `BD-E-EH`, `BD-E-LIFT-SUBSET`,
`BD-E-DEVICE-ADDRESS`, `BD-E-REGION-UNBOUNDED`, `BD-E-REGION-EXTERNAL`, `BD-E-REGION-THUNK`,
`BD-E-REGION-TIER`, `BD-E-REVERSIBLE-MARKER` (except measurement), `BD-E-INPLACE-UNVERIFIED`,
`BD-E-OPERAND-UNBOUNDED`, `BD-E-HANDLE-STORE`, and `BD-E-REGION-TYPE` for oracle candidates.

Fault code 4 (§17.5) with the named diagnostic: `T-DEAD`, `T-FOREIGN`, `T-CLEAN`, `T-INDEX`,
`T-ALIAS`, `T-THETA`, `T-MEASURE`, `T-SIZE`, `T-CTRL`, `T-LEAK`, `T-REGION`, `T-REGION-INVALID`,
`T-OP`, `T-CLASS`, `T-IFACE`, `T-BUDGET`, `T-TAINT-HOST`. Deleted: `T-BORROW` (outside generations
`qborrow` is a clean allocation, bd `.9`), `T-TIER` (§7.4).

### 22.4 Warnings

`BD-W-QSTORE-UNCLEAN` (§6.7), `BD-W-TAINT-HOST` (TDL-11). A
warning never changes a verdict.

## 23. Versioning

- The **full version string** of this document (`0.2.12`) is the unit of compatibility: a plugin
  implements exactly one dialect version and targets exactly one ISA version (§19.9); drafts are
  not compatible with each other. [deps: DLT-23, DLT-19.9]
- The consumer-gap dispositions revise the full dialect to `0.2.12`. [deps: DLT-5.2, DLT-12.2-P11, DLT-23]
  The lifting map remains `0.2.9` (global-numbering correction); `norm`, `handles` and
  `regions` remain `0.2.8`; unchanged oracle-abi, tdl and effects remain `0.2.4`, marker/header,
  asm and corpus remain `0.2`, platform and driver remain `0.2.1`. Every reference object is checked
  again; unchanged ISA-0.8 interface signatures retain their hashes. The map version
  is recorded in certificate metadata, not inserted into ISA-owned interface signatures.
- Every semantic change bumps the version; the reference objects and hashes of §16 depend on the
  ISA version string, the map and the layout, and change with any of them (0.1's hashes changed
  with the wrapper form of §16.2).
- The build record (§19.9) records the runtimes, arith and asm-table hashes and the budget-table
  hash; `SPEC_VERSIONS.toml` records `dialect` and the targeted `isa`; the change rules are in
  `spec/README.md`.

Only mechanics appear here. Each row states the capability it keeps; none has a default of
reject, refuse, fault or no effect for something the north star promises.

|---|---|---|---|
| D12 | host-stack addresses passed into device code (NS §3.2 "where possible") | executions they influence are layout-dependent (§18.7) and validated against the reference interpreter | every program runs; only the comparison oracle changes |

| D23 | ThinLTO with the plugin | full LTO: one merged module | every program merges into one module (audit D23, M) |

| D26 | the binary layout of descriptors and budget records beyond the C view | the LLVM struct layout under the module's datalayout (§8.1, §9.1) | every target |
| D27 | budget defaults (§9.2) | 1048576 steps, 1048576 heap bytes, 65536 stack cells, depth 256, `sstack` 65536 | every budget is overridable |

| D31 | target ABI rows for `va_list` (§14.3), asm (§15) and target intrinsics beyond x86-64, AArch64 and RISC-V64 | added with the target | the three targets of the rungs |

## 25. Blockers and ISA items

### 25.1 Blockers

A **blocker** is a capability the dialect fully specifies whose stated case has no realisation on
ISA 0.8. BLK-3 has been retired by §8.7. Unlike the original outline list,
BLK-5 can affect typed fault points in R-tier rungs; successful executions remain covered.
The maintainer ruled
(2026-10-29) that ISA 0.9 waits until this dialect is council-accepted.

| Id | Code | NS capability | Affected case | ISA 0.8 realisation that exists | Exact ISA change |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **BLK-1** = ISA-4 | `BD-B-ISA4` | NS §3.1 handle tables; §4.1, §4.2 dynamic allocation ("real allocation"); Bennett.jl vectors of qubits; adjoints of generations whose register set is known only at run time | allocation sites or entry parameter/result roots whose handles persist across entries without a finite instance bound (§6.5); register widths with value set ⊤ (§3.4); a Co width with an unbounded value set (TDL-10) | direct `qreg` values for handles that stay out of memory; static register files with dispatch for bounded sites (§6.5) | (i) a dynamic-width type `qreg[*]`; `q = qreg.new cls n` with n an `i64` value yields `qreg[*]`; `w = qreg.width q`. (ii) Binding a `qreg[*]` to a `wire[n]` or `region[A,W]` slot checks the width at the call: CC trap, R fault `FAULT_UB_TRAP`, on a mismatch. (iii) `id = qreg.id q` (non-consuming; the §10 launch id) and `q = qreg.from_id id` (yields `qreg[*]`; a trap if the id is not live, is viewed or is consumed). History frames: `qreg.id` is pure; `qreg.from_id` records the handle reference |
| **BLK-2** = ISA-1 | `BD-B-ISA1` | NS §4.2 "run-time budgets come from the host or from measurement results" | budget fields computed on the device (§9.5 case 3) | pre-minted finite sets; stub-minted copies of launch arguments (§9.5) | a CC instruction `tk = budget.mint @region s h st d` (`i64` operands) with the ISA §5.4 validation of `bennett_budget` as a CC trap; the token is scoped to the run; an impure history frame recording the token |
| **BLK-5** = ISA-7 | `BD-B-ISA7` | NS §4.2 named fault outcomes and R reversible prefixes | every R entry (§11.5), including mandatory budget codes 1/2/3 and explicit __bennett_nz_fault(C); direct calls under §7.4 excluded | CC hostcall reports C; generic R trap stops execution but does not satisfy typed reporting | R `fault C`: terminate with fault record code C, no later source effect, retain reversible prefix history; specify the implicit trap-cause-to-code table (div0 5, overflow 6, memory 7, trap 4, allocation failure 2) |
| **BLK-4** = new ISA-12 (audit B-38) | `BD-B-ISA12` | NS §4.2 quantum allocation inside reversible-tier code | `qalloc`/`qborrow`/`qfree` in an R closure with no finite instance bound (§7.5b); entry-state transfer from R code (§8.5) | hoisting to the CC caller with a static count (§7.5b) | permit `qreg.new`, `qreg.free`, `qreg.slice` and `qreg.concat` in R; the R history records them; the reversal of `qreg.new` is `qreg.free` with the cleanliness obligation (simulator check; hardware per ISA §17 item 14); the reversal of `qreg.free` is `qreg.new` (clean); `measure` stays CC-only |

BLK-5's checked witnesses are `r-fault-code` (explicit code 2) and
`r-budget-prefix` (the pre-instruction code 1 boundary). Their verdict is
`blocked:BD-B-ISA7` when grading against ISA 0.8. The exact change is now
specified in the ISA 0.9 draft, commit `61550b4` (section 0.2 K5 and section
4.1 `fault C`); its presence in that draft does not change this draft's ISA target.

On ISA 0.8 a module has verdict blocked:BD-B-ISA7 if its device program contains
an R-tier variant reached as an R entry: a reversible region candidate invoked outside
generation, or the §19.2 whole-program R entry. Every such variant has the §17.6 budget
check, regardless of supplied run-time limits. A reversible call treated as a direct call
under §7.4 is not an R entry and does not by itself block. Other rejection/blocker
precedence remains §1.6. The R blocker trap is generic ISA trap with no hostcall, reached
only on executions that would report a typed fault (budget exhaustion, F(C), nz_fault(C));
all other executions are unaffected. This fallback does not implement typed reporting.

**Blocker report** (one line per blocker point, in processing order, in the outputs, §19.9):
`<code> <function> <value or call>: <reason>; <ISA 0.8 constraint>; <BLK id> (<ISA item>) <change>`.

**Blocker trap.** A blocked program is compiled (§1.6) with each blocker point lowered to the ISA
0.8 realisation that exists for the bounded cases (the static file of §6.5 with D29's size, the
pre-minted budgets of the value set, the specialised pairs) and, on an execution that falls
outside them, CC uses `hostcall bennett.fault` with code 4 and the blocker's code as payload, then
`trap` (§11.5). R uses generic `trap` without hostcall, with the static blocker report naming
the dependency; exact typed R reporting requires ISA 0.9 K5. So only the executions the blocker affects fail, and they fail loud.

### 25.2 Wording items

*(non-normative)* The following are the retained historical ISA 0.8 wording
requests, not an adoption of ISA 0.9 operations or a claim of normal return after
an R fault; outline-0.3 I-gwf and I-bz6 record the terminal-run replacement for
the 0.3 track.

No capability is lost; two implementations could differ until each is fixed in ISA 0.9:

- **ISA-7** is a capability dependency blocker, BLK-5 (§25.1), not a wording-only item.
- **ISA-8**: an R call returns a fault status to its CC caller; runs ending in an R fault are
  reverse-eligible.
- **ISA-3 editorial**: the JCS spelling of `args` types: the ISA §16.2 type spelling as JSON strings
  (`"i64"`), as §16 uses.
- **`qreg.slice`**: whether `lo` may be a run-time value (§10 TDL-4 uses literal offsets only).
- **`qreg` across CC calls**: ISA §3.2 places handles in the classical environment, but the ISA
  §3.3 transition table is silent on CC `call`, `ret` and `br` block parameters of `qreg` type;
  §6.5 relies on them carrying `qreg` values unchanged.
- **ISA §5.4 code 10** "(R only)" versus `fault 10` in oracles (§11.3; ISA §4.2 admits every C <
  2^F).
- **Certificate fields** for the build record hash (§19.9), the budget-table hash and the
  `deferred_measurement` flag (§7.5a); meanwhile `producer.version` build metadata and
  `definedness_assumptions` carry them.
- **ISA §12.2 domains with a fixed classical region**: NS §8 rung 4(a) verifies the oracle "over
  all rows and all target values" with the database fixed classically before entry; ISA §12.2
  labels a domain that fixes an `in` region's cells as evidence with a covered fraction. The ISA
  should say whether an exhaustive claim over the remaining components, with the fixed region
  prepared from classical bytes (`qprep`, `bennett_qreg_prepare`), is a proof for that region
  content.

Certificates MUST retain build-record and resolved-table identities and deferred-measurement information in the existing documented metadata channel where no dedicated field exists, and a fixed-region strict subdomain MUST be labelled evidence with its covered fraction by the checker, treating a producer label as a checked request. [deps: DLT-19.9, DLT-25.2, DLT-18.7, ISA-12.1, ISA-12.2, ISA-12.3]

*(non-normative, I-gwf / D22 migration ledger).* The full-invocation and
explicit-expected-relation binding proposal remains in outline-0.3 I-gwf for
ISA 0.9 adoption; its K74 checker-owned label rule supplies the wording above.
No dedicated schema field, 0.9 object, or terminal R status contract is adopted
on this ISA 0.8 target. Bead gwf remains open for that versioned migration.

### 25.3 Efficiency items

Correct on ISA 0.8 by the stated construction; ISA 0.9 candidates on evidence only:

| Item | Construction on ISA 0.8 |
|---|---|
| ISA-3 parameterised qinplace | CC-level oracle/swap/inverse expansion (§8.7); BLK-3 retired |
| ISA-5 (two simultaneous slices of one `qreg`) | per-wire registers, or a quantum-addressed gate |
| ISA-9 (borrowing outside static Q regions) | clean allocation for CC-spanning borrows (§18.3) |
| ISA-11 (host memory by reference) | promotion (§19.4) |
| G-12 (oracle entry state at native addresses) | entry-state transfer by replay (§8.5) |
| G-13 (interface-region base at call time) | a static arena (TDL-4) |
| G-14 (predicated step machine in actions) | unrolled padded events (TDL-10) |
| G-15 (payloads wider than 128 bits) | 2k − 1 oracle calls (§8.6) |
| G-16 (fibers and interrupts in CC/R) | rewindable frames (§14.6) and poll points (§20.4) |
| SQLite E1–E5 | per-function cell widths, frame width, an indirect-call row, a block-copy row, a classical-content hint for `in` regions |

ISA-2 is not a gap (the R step counter, §17.6). ISA-6 is not a gap (fresh one-wire copy and measurement, §18.3). ISA-10 is settled by the ISA's test vectors. **LLVM changes needed**: none; the legality
findings' LLVM bugs are outside the envelope (§21.1).

## Core Rules
- **Default**: Use beads for ALL task tracking (`bd create`, `bd ready`, `bd close`)
- **Prohibited**: Do NOT use TodoWrite, TaskCreate, or markdown files for task tracking
- **Workflow**: Create beads issue BEFORE writing code, mark in_progress when starting
- **Memory**: Use `bd remember "insight"` for persistent knowledge across sessions. Do NOT use MEMORY.md files — they fragment across accounts. Search with `bd memories <keyword>`.
- Persistence you don't need beats lost context
- Default: do not commit, push, or run dolt remote sync without explicit authority. Team-maintainer behavior is opt-in and still subordinate to user/orchestrator instructions.
- Git workflow: conservative by default; commit/push only with explicit user/orchestrator or team-maintainer authority
- Session management: check `bd ready` for available work

## Essential Commands

### Finding Work
- `bd ready` - Show issues ready to work (no blockers)
- `bd list --status=open` - All open issues
- `bd list --status=in_progress` - Your active work
- `bd show <id>` - Detailed issue view with dependencies

### Creating & Updating
- `bd create --title="Summary of this issue" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task|bug|feature --priority=2` - New issue
  - Priority: 0-4 or P0-P4 (0=critical, 2=medium, 4=backlog). NOT "high"/"medium"/"low"
- `bd create ... --parent=<id>` - Hierarchical child (task under epic, subtask under task; inherits parent labels)
- `bd update <id> --claim` - Claim work
- `bd unclaim <id>` - Release stuck issue (agent crashed)
- `bd update <id> --assignee=username` - Assign to someone
- `bd update <id> --if-assignee=<expected> --assignee=<new>` - Atomic reassign: applies only if the assignee still matches (--if-status=<expected> guards status; --if-assignee='' requires unassigned). Mismatch exits non-zero with nothing written — never retry blindly
- `bd update <id> --title/--description/--notes/--design` - Update fields inline
- `bd close <id>` - Mark complete
- `bd close <id1> <id2> ...` - Close multiple issues at once (more efficient)
- `bd close <id> --reason="explanation"` - Close with reason
- **Tip**: When creating multiple issues/tasks/epics, use parallel subagents for efficiency
- **WARNING**: Do NOT use `bd edit` - it opens $EDITOR (vim/nano) which blocks agents

### Dependencies & Blocking
- `bd dep add <issue> <depends-on>` - Add dependency (issue depends on depends-on)
- `bd blocked` - Show all blocked issues
- `bd show <id>` - See what's blocking/blocked by this issue

### Sync & Collaboration
- `bd search <query>` - Search issues by keyword

### Project Health
- `bd stats` - Project statistics (open/closed/blocked counts)
- `bd doctor` - Check for issues (sync problems, missing hooks)
- `bd doctor --check=conventions` - Check for convention drift (lint, stale, orphans)

### Quality Tools
- `bd create --validate` - Check description has required sections
- `bd create --acceptance="criteria"` - Set acceptance criteria (checked by --validate)
- `bd create --design="decisions"` - Record design decisions
- `bd create --notes="context"` - Add supplementary notes
- `bd config set validation.on-create warn` - Auto-validate on every create
- `bd lint` - Check existing issues for missing sections

### Lifecycle & Hygiene
- `bd defer <id> --until="date"` - Defer work to a future date
- `bd supersede <id> --with=<new-id>` - Mark issue as superseded
- `bd close <id> --suggest-next` - Show newly unblocked issues after closing
- `bd stale` - Find issues with no recent activity
- `bd orphans` - Find issues with broken dependencies
- `bd preflight` - Pre-PR checks (lint, stale, orphans)

- `bd mol pour <name>` - Start structured workflow from formula

An R or L variant never makes a host call (ISA §4.1: `hostcall` is CC only; NS §4.2: coherent

## 5. The device analysis (one fixpoint)

The device analysis is a whole-program, flow-insensitive, inclusion-based (Andersen-style),
field-sensitive points-to and value-set analysis over every device variant and every host
function that exchanges data with one (passes it a pointer, or shares a global it accesses). It
is specified exactly: **every verdict, candidate set and dispatch domain of this document is
computed from the reference analysis below.** A plugin MAY use a more precise analysis to shrink
dispatch domains or arena placement in its lowering, never to change a verdict (the run-time
value is always a member of the reference set, so the results are unchanged).

### 5.1 Abstract objects and atoms

The **abstract objects** are:

- each `alloca` instruction;
- each global variable;
- each **allocation site**: a call of a function that carries `allockind("alloc")` or
  `allocsize(...)`, or whose name is one of `malloc`, `calloc`, `realloc`, `aligned_alloc`,
  `posix_memalign` (the object is the one written through its first argument), `memalign`,
  `strdup`, `strndup`, `_Znwm`, `_Znam`, `_ZnwmRKSt9nothrow_t`, `_ZnamRKSt9nothrow_t`,
  `_ZnwmSt11align_val_t`, `_ZnamSt11align_val_t`, `__rust_alloc`, `__rust_alloc_zeroed`,
  `__rust_realloc`, `julia.gc_alloc_obj`, `ijl_gc_alloc_typed`, `jl_gc_alloc`,
  `ijl_gc_small_alloc`, `ijl_gc_big_alloc`, or `__bennett_nz_alloc`;
- the pseudo-object `EXT` (memory of unknown provenance: host memory reached through a pointer
  the analysis did not create).

Heap objects are context-insensitive: one object per allocation-site instruction of the variant
in which it occurs. The list above is the reference list; `bennett-runtimes` declares its
allocator entry points with `allockind("alloc")` so that they are covered by the first rule.

An **offset** is an integer byte offset, a **residue class** (r mod s)[lo, hi) with s ≥ 2 (every
offset o with o ≡ r (mod s) and lo ≤ o < hi), or `*` (any offset). An **atom** is one of:

- `(X, o)`: a pointer into object X at offset o;
- `FN(F)`: the address A(F) of a defined function F;
- `ROOT(r)`: a handle with root r (the allocation site of a `qalloc`/`qborrow` call, §6.5);
- `INT(c)`: the integer constant c (of the value's width).

Every SSA value v (per lane for vectors, per index path for aggregates) has an **abstract
value** AV(v) = (A, t): a finite set A of atoms and a flag t; t = ⊤ means "and any other integer
or address". ⊤ never stands for a `ROOT` atom (handle provenance is always explicit); for a
function-pointer operand it stands for "any function of the operand's type whose address is
used" (§7.2, §14.2). Every slot (X, o) of memory has a content AV(X, o) of the same form. When A
holds more than **K = 4096** `INT` atoms they are dropped and t is set (a normative constant: it
decides dispatch and blocker verdicts, so §24 does not defer it). "AV(v) = ⊤" below means t is
set; unions and inclusions are taken component-wise.

Abstract-object and return propagation MUST cover attributed allocator calls and defined allocator wrappers through their underlying allocation sites without requiring the wrapper's name in a whitelist. [deps: DLT-5.1-P4, DLT-5.1-P6, DLT-5.2-P10, ISA-7.1, ISA-7.2]

### 5.2 Rules

Before the fixpoint, seed the initialiser atoms of every global, mutable or constant,
into its slots. For each alloca and allocation-site object, also represent every extent
whose initial contents are zero under §§17.1/17.3 as a zero-byte initial image; represent a
nonconstant extent symbolically over its possible allocated bytes. Subsequent stores union
additional possibilities and never erase these initial possibilities. This is an initial
image, not a claim that the object remains immutable.
The analysis is the least fixpoint of the following rules over AV. "Copies" means AV(result) ⊇
AV(operand) lane by lane; ⊤ absorbs. S2 models the value effects of the specified
normalisation before verdicts and dispatch domains freeze; pre-normalisation syntax does
not permit omitting a value produced by the materialised execution.

| Rule | Instruction or source | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| PT1 | `alloca`, global `@G`, allocation-site call | AV ∋ (X, 0); zero-filled initial extents are seeded as above |
| PT2 | `getelementptr T, ptr p, idx…` | uses P(a), I(a) and the complete finite-width computation of §16.4, also for constant expressions: sequential indices are sign-extended/truncated to I(a), scaling and offset accumulation wrap modulo 2^I(a), and addition preserves pointer bits above I(a). Zero accumulated offset copies AV(p). For every (X,o), include the image of all represented base addresses and index values (unknown base bits range over patterns consistent with DataLayout and X's alignment), using integer/residue-class/`*` offset unions. Unbounded mathematical addition is not a substitute. If an address may leave X, include (EXT, `*`) and the non-handle top flag; PT6 propagates this unknown-address alternative. The unknown-address alternative does not by itself establish host escape (§6.4): retain the original base provenance for that test; actual host/unknown origins keep their restrictions. ROOT provenance and §6.2 restrictions remain explicit. Array dimensions determine stride, never an index bound |
| PT3 | `phi`, `select` (value operands), `freeze`, `addrspacecast`; `bitcast` | the first four copy (union). A bitcast copies lane by lane only when source and destination have identical lane counts and per-lane representation widths, treating a scalar as one lane. Otherwise use the virtual N21 bitcast expansion of §12.6: pack source-lane bit patterns into its prescribed bit string in DataLayout order, then resegment at the destination representation width. Compute output AV through PT4/PT5 transfers of that expansion; finite INT lane sets denote their full Cartesian product, and K applies per resulting lane. Never copy a source-lane INT atom as the value of a packed destination scalar. Unknown bits propagate non-handle top through those transfers. Explicit object and ROOT provenance follows the same expansion and remains subject to §6.2; top never substitutes for ROOT. Apply also to constant expressions and fixed-vscale forms during S2 before verdicts, candidate sets, shapes and dispatch domains freeze. Direct finite bit-string evaluation is equivalent; no LLVM optimisation is required |
| PT4 | `ptrtoint`, `inttoptr`, `zext`/`sext`/`trunc` | copy only bit-preserving conversions. Otherwise apply the actual source/destination-width bit-vector conversion (§16.4): ptrtoint/inttoptr zero-extend or truncate at P(a), sext sign-extends. Use PT2’s represented-address image construction, retaining originating object and ROOT provenance. A narrowed representation that cannot be reassociated with its original object adds (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤; numerical uncertainty is not host escape. Apply also to constant expressions |
| PT5 | `add`/`sub` of a value with pointer atoms and an `INT` set | computes address bits modulo 2^W at the integer operation’s width W, never unbounded offset addition. Relative displacements from the originating base are modular too: zero net displacement represents that base again, including after an inttoptr truncation to P(a). Use PT2’s represented-address image construction at W bits (full-width arithmetic, no preserved high GEP bits), retaining original object and ROOT provenance. Keep modular displacement alternatives even outside the allocation, and additionally include (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤ when an image may leave it. Residue classes and `*` take the same finite-width image; an unrepresentable image retains (X, `*`) for each originating X plus (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤. Other integer operations on pointer atoms use that conservative image. Widening a pointer-derived integer by zext/sext uses that conservative image too (fixed reference fallback); ptrtoint zero-extension retains its base representation. Numerical uncertainty does not itself establish host escape (PT2). The rules apply also to constant expressions, before candidate/dispatch domains freeze; on finite `INT` sets, every LLVM integer operation, cast and `icmp` is evaluated elementwise; with x ⊤ and a constant m, `and x, m` gives the submasks of m and `urem x, m` gives {0, …, m−1} (each still subject to K); a range attribute or `!range` metadata removed by N3/N4 is not a bound on materialised execution and MUST NOT restrict any verdict, candidate set, descriptor shape or dispatch domain. Only bounds independently established by materialised instructions restrict the set; otherwise retain ⊤ and apply the ordinary operation rules, including finite masks/remainders |
| PT6 | `load T, ptr p` | for each (X,o) ∈ AV(p): memory atoms carry their storage interval and representation width; copy a whole-value atom only for that identical interval with a compatible representation. A known immutable byte image decodes exactly the accessed bytes using DataLayout and T (including initialisers). For a partial, spanning or overlapping access whose result bits are not represented exactly, the non-handle value is ⊤, never the union of component INT values. X=EXT gives ⊤. A non-handle top flag on p contributes the same unknown-address load alternative even when explicit object offsets overlap no known slot; it MUST NOT be dropped. ROOT provenance remains explicit and §6.2's partial-handle restrictions still apply. For a possibly valid load wholly contained in a zero-filled initial extent, include the all-zero value of the load’s representation and width, unioned with values from every overlapping store or copy under these interval rules. PT8 propagates these initial byte possibilities when copying memory. Zero contributes no ROOT. Region inputs, replayed caller bytes and other nonzero-initialised contents retain their specified sources; this rule does not replace them with zeros |
| PT7 | `store v, ptr p` | retain each written interval and representation width; partial writes overlapping a larger load participate in PT6's conservative fallback. AV(X, o + d_k) ⊇ AV(v_k) for each (X, o) ∈ AV(p) and each lane or field k at byte offset d_k |
| PT8 | memory copy (`llvm.memcpy`/`memmove`/`memcpy.inline`, `__bennett_rt_memmove`) of (dst, src, len); memory fill (`llvm.memset`, `__bennett_rt_memset`) of (dst, v, len) | copy: propagate zero-byte initial images over the copied byte ranges, including their symbolic extents; for each slot (Y, s) of each (Y, o_s) ∈ AV(src) and (Z, o_d) ∈ AV(dst): when offsets and len are constants and the atom's entire storage interval [s, s + size(atom)) lies in the copied range, copy its atom and representation width to (Z, o_d + s − o_s). Every partially overlapping atom instead contributes non-handle ⊤ and explicit ROOT provenance to the affected destination interval (unknown offsets/len use `*`); do not copy the whole component INT. Fill: every slot in [o_d, o_d + len) of each (Z, o_d) ∈ AV(dst) (all of Z when an offset or len is not constant) gets the `INT` atoms of the byte v repeated to the slot's width (⊤ when v is not constant) |
| PT9 | direct call of G, or indirect call with candidate G (§14.2) | AV(parameter i of G) ⊇ AV(argument i); AV(result) ⊇ the union of AV over G's `ret` operands; `byval` arguments copy the pointee's slots into the callee's copy object |
| PT10 | `insertelement`/`extractelement`/`shufflevector`/`insertvalue`/`extractvalue` | extraction/insertion use N15’s total unsigned index semantics. Constant in-range extraction copies its lane; constant out-of-range extraction gives the element type’s zero. Dynamic extraction unions every source lane selected by a represented index, plus zero iff the index set includes an unsigned value >= L (including top when 2^K > L). No zero is added if every represented index is in range; zero adds no ROOT. In-range insertion replaces the selected lane; out-of-range insertion preserves the vector. Dynamic insertion includes each old lane unless it is certainly replaced, and the inserted value whenever that lane can be selected. Valid shuffle indices and aggregate paths move atoms unchanged (handle-table reads, audit B-23) |
| PT11 | a parameter of a function with a host caller, a device entry, or a function whose address host code uses | AV ⊇ {(EXT, `*`)} for `ptr` parameters, ⊤ for integer parameters, plus the atoms of the device-side arguments by PT9; for a pointer parameter i of a device entry E, also the candidate atom ROOT(E, i) of §6.6 |
| PT12 | a `qalloc`/`qborrow` call; a call of a function with no definition that resolves to a host call (§4.5) or is trapped; an `atomicrmw`/`cmpxchg` (before N9) | `qalloc`/`qborrow`: AV ∋ ROOT(site) (§6.5). An undefined callee: the result is ⊤ and every slot reachable from its pointer arguments gets ⊤. Before domains freeze, PT12 MUST apply PT6 to the old value, the PT5 transfers of the full N9 update, and PT7 to the actual stored value, with cmpxchg returning its old-value and success fields and storing the selected new-or-old value. [deps: DLT-5.2-P1, DLT-5.2-P6, DLT-5.2-P7, DLT-5.2-P8, DLT-5.2-P13, DLT-12.2-P11, ISA-4.1, ISA-4.2] |

An `inttoptr` of a constant gives (EXT, `*`) and, for function-pointer purposes, ⊤: function
```text state=compatible-allocator-graph at=return
{"roots":{"%from_node":["top%a"],"%from_callback":["top%a","top%b"],"%lane":["top%a","top%b"]},"counter_roots":[],"native":[[9,2,4,2,0,0],[9,2,4,2,0,0]]}
```

### 5.3 Value sets

The **value set** VS(v) of an integer or pointer operand is the set of its `INT` and `FN` atoms
(pointer atoms denote addresses that are not compile-time constants and make VS(v) = ⊤ for
dispatch purposes), or ⊤. A value set is a precision tool only:

- an operand with a finite VS is dispatched over it (§3.4, §14.2);
- an operand with VS = ⊤ is dispatched over its type's whole valid domain where that domain is
  finite: operation codes and classes (§3.4), binding kinds (§8.4), the function addresses of a
  type (§7.2, §14.2);
- otherwise it is a blocker case of §25.1: widths (BLK-1), budgets computed on the device
  (BLK-2), parameters of in-place pairs (§8.7).

### 5.4 Uses

| Result | Read by |
|---|---|
| `FN` atoms of region operands | region candidate sets (§7.2) |
| `FN` atoms of indirect callees | indirect-call candidate sets (§14.2) |
| `ROOT` atoms | handle provenance (§6.2), handle containment (§6.4), static register files (§6.5) |
| `INT` atoms of descriptor and budget fields | descriptor shapes (§8.4), budget pre-minting (§9.5) |
| slots stored under control | the may-Co analysis and arena placement (§10, TDL-2, TDL-4) |
| objects loaded by L code | the oracle read set R and entry-state transfer (§8.5) |
| `INT` atoms of `op`, `cls`, `n` | dispatch domains (§3.4) |

  %s = alloca %struct.tab
  %h0 = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 1)
  store ptr %h0, ptr %s

**Standing rule (CLAUDE.md rule 10; council rubric S0).** This document narrows no capability
that the north star promises (its thesis, §3–§5 and every rung of the §8 demo ladder; Grover
over unmodified SQLite is the core). It rejects at compile time only what the north star itself
requires to be rejected or what a maintainer decision settles; every such rejection is a row of
§22.1 citing the clause or decision. A deferral (§24) postpones mechanics only and states the
capability it keeps. Where ISA 0.8 cannot express a promised capability in some case, the
dialect still specifies the whole mechanism, states the ISA 0.8 realisation that exists for the
other cases, and names a blocker (§25) whose verdict is `blocked:`, never `reject:`.

B1–B17, questions E1–E14).

## 1. Scope and conformance

### 1.1 What the dialect is

The dialect is the contract between **front ends** that emit LLVM IR containing calls to
`__bennett_*` marker functions and the **plugin** that, at the final link of a whole program,
recognises those calls, analyses and normalises the code they reach, and maps it onto the ISA
(ISA §1.1). It fixes:

- the marker ABI, operand encodings and C header (§3);
- the processing order, execution tiers, polyvariant variants and function addresses (§4);
- the device analysis: points-to, value sets and every question answered from them (§5);
- the handle convention, provenance, comparison and collections of handles (§6);
- regions: kinds, candidate sets, dispatch, identity, well-formedness (§7);
- the oracle interface: the descriptor, parameter and device bindings, entry state, wide outputs
  and in-place updates (§8);
- budgets (§9);
- taint-driven lifting of classical code under control (§10);
- effects and device models per tier (§11);
- the materialised normalisation and the residual-poison validator (§12), floating point
  (§13), calls and control transfer (§14), inline asm and target intrinsics (§15);
- the lifting map from materialised LLVM to ISA objects (§16);
- the device classical memory model, CC and R lowering rules and fault outcomes (§17);
- a reference semantics from marker programs to the ISA formal model (§18);
- the driver's modes and plugin-invocation interface (§19);
- the platform profile `riscv64-bennett-virt` (§20);
- the legality corpus and conformance suites (§21); diagnostics (§22); versioning (§23);
  deferred mechanics (§24); blockers and ISA items (§25).

It does not fix the plugin's optimisation pipeline after normalisation, the plugin's device
objects beyond the constraints of §18.7, or any format the ISA owns.

### 1.2 Conformance classes

| Class | Who | Obligation |
|---|---|---|
| Producer | front ends (clang, rustc, Bennett.jl, importers) | emit modules that satisfy §3 and the outlining conventions of §7.6; never emit a reserved name of §3.1 other than a marker |
| Plugin | `bennett-plugin` and competitors | the processing order of §4.1; give every module the verdict the rules give it, with the diagnostics of §22; produce ISA objects whose execution equals the reference semantics (§18.7); suites DA–DX (§21.3) |
| Driver | `bennett-driver` | §19; links `bennett-runtimes` and `bennett-arith` bitcode of the link's LLVM major (§19.1); builds the comparison link and the native comparison binary (§17.7); emits blocker reports (§25) |
| Validator | the plugin's validator and `bennett-conformance` | the residual-poison validator (§12.4) and the reference recogniser (§3, §5, §6, §7), implemented independently; suites DA–DN |
| Runtime | `bennett-runtimes` and `bennett-arith` | define every entry point that §11, §13, §14, §17 and §20 name (prefixes `__bennett_rt_`, `__bennett_dev_`, `__bennett_sf_`, §3.1) with the semantics stated there, as ordinary bitcode compiled by the same pipeline; suites DR and DF |
| Platform | a platform profile of §20 | a board model plus shims satisfying §20; suite DP |

`scripts/dialect_check.py` in this repository is the seed of the pre-flight checker (§1.5). It
never bounds this document: an example it cannot yet execute fails the check (rule P-2, §1.5).

### 1.3 Relation to the ISA specification

The ISA is settled; this document never redefines it. Every ISA notion used here (tiers, wires,
`qreg` handles, oracles, actions, permutation actions, lprocs, F_B, budgets, tokens, fault
codes, the reference interpreter, interface and closure hashes, layouts, the launch ABI) has
exactly the meaning ISA §2–§17 give it. Where the dialect needs a construct ISA 0.8 lacks, this
document states (a) the full dialect mechanism, (b) its realisation on ISA 0.8 where one exists,
which may be a longer construction (an **efficiency item**, §25.3), and (c) otherwise a
**blocker** (§25.1) naming the exact ISA change. The dialect never shrinks to fit the ISA and
never extends the ISA unilaterally.

### 1.4 Departures from the north star

Only these remain. Every row of 0.1 §1.4 that narrowed scope (1.4-d, 1.4-e, 1.4-f) is deleted;
its replacement is §10, §9 and §8.7 respectively.

| North star | Dialect 0.2 | Why |
|---|---|---|
| §3.1 handle row: a handle through an `inttoptr`/`ptrtoint` round trip is rejected | Provenance flows through bitwise copies (§6.2) | Spike F1; maintainer decision 2; north-star amendment A2 (drafted with A1, bd `-25h`) |
| §3.1: regions entered as `__bennett_ctrl(q, @f, …)` with a constant `@f` | Region operands are classical data dispatched over a candidate set bounded by the program (§7.2) | Spike F3; maintainer decision 3 |
| Region named by symbol | Region identity is by content (§7.3) | Spike F2; maintainer decision 1 |
| §3.1 handle row is silent on comparison | `icmp eq/ne` of handles is identity (§6.3) | Maintainer decision 4 (2026-10-29; legality findings §3); a superset |
| §3.1, §4.2: a `qstore` into a register not known to be clean is a compile-time error | `qstore` is the ISA's XOR store; the compile-time rule is an optional warning `BD-W-QSTORE-UNCLEAN` (§6.7) | A register-granularity rule forbids the uncomputing second store; a superset of the north star (audit 1.4-g, D7) |
| §3.2: normalisation of "the outlined region" | Normalisation applies to every device variant (§12.1) | Device CC code is lowered to ISA CC, whose semantics differs from LLVM's poison semantics (ISA §4.1); a superset (audit 1.4-h) |

be recycled while an observable stale copy can remain; integer wrap is not permission
to alias generations. Copies continue to denote their original generation after free. ISA 0.8 has no instruction that
reads a `qreg`'s launch id (BLK-1's `qreg.id` would add one), so tags are the plugin's. At the
host–device boundary a handle is the ISA launch register id (`bennett_qreg`, ISA §10), which the
launch binds to a `qreg` parameter or result (§6.6). Neither value may influence a result other
than through §6.3; the rules below make them unobservable. A handle
denotes a register of n wires, n the `qalloc` operand; wire i of handle h is written h.i.

### 6.2 Provenance (maintainer decision 2)

The **provenance** of a value is the set of `ROOT` atoms of its abstract value (§5). Provenance
flows through **bitwise copies** only: `ptrtoint`/`inttoptr` at width pw, integer loads and
stores of width pw, vector lanes (including dynamic lanes, PT10: a dynamically indexed vector of
handles is a handle table), aggregate fields, memory copies, `phi`, `select`, `freeze`, call
arguments and returns (PT3–PT10). A handle-derived value (any lane or field) MAY be:

1. a handle operand of a marker (§3.2);
2. copied by the rules above, including storage in any slot the analysis tracks (§6.4);
3. an operand of `icmp eq` or `icmp ne` (§6.3);
4. an argument of a direct or indirect call of a defined function, or a return value.

Every other use is `BD-E-HANDLE-USE` (maintainer decision 2: "any other use of a
handle-derived integer is rejected fail-loud"; NS §3.1 "never dereferenced"): in particular
arithmetic, bitwise operations, `icmp` with any other predicate, any cast other than the copies
above (`trunc`, `zext`, `sext`, `bitcast` to a non-pointer-width type, `ptrtoint` to a width ≠
pw), `getelementptr` with a handle as base or index, `switch` on it, use as the pointer operand of
a `load`, `store`, `atomicrmw`, `cmpxchg` or memory intrinsic (dereference), and a load of any
type whose byte range overlaps a handle-derived slot without being exactly one pw-wide lane of
it (a partial read).

A handle operand of a marker whose provenance is empty is `BD-E-HANDLE-UNTRACKED` (it cannot be
a handle of this program). *(non-normative)* A tracked value that at run time is not a live
handle (a stale copy, a freed register) traps at the marker (`T-DEAD`, §18.3).

Dynamically indexed handle vectors MUST use PT10 lane provenance and N15 total index semantics, retaining explicit ROOT atoms and adding zero only for possible out-of-range extraction. [deps: DLT-5.2-P11, DLT-6.2-P1, DLT-12.2-P17, ISA-4.1, ISA-4.2]

### 6.3 Comparison (maintainer decision 4)

`icmp eq` and `icmp ne` of a handle with `null`, or with another handle-derived value, compare
**identity**: a live handle is never null, and two handles are equal iff they denote the same
allocation generation, including when comparing a freed identity with a later allocation
in the same physical slot. The result is not handle-derived. In CC it lowers to `icmp` on register tags. This
covers C `if (!q)` and Rust's `Option<Handle>` niche (audit D8). *(non-normative)* A comparison
is a classical observation of identity, not of the register's content; ids are unique, so it
equals the native comparison of the two handle values.

Handle equality and null tests MUST compare allocation identity, preserve stale-versus-new inequality and produce a result without handle provenance. [deps: DLT-6.1-P1, DLT-6.3-P1, ISA-3.3-P7, ISA-4.1-P4]

### 6.4 Collections and containment

Handles may be stored in any slot the analysis tracks: tables, vectors, linked structures,
globals (field-sensitively), heap objects, and objects passed to indirect calls or returned by
address-taken functions (audit B-22, B-24, B-26, B-29). A handle may **escape** only where code
the analysis cannot see could read it:

- `BD-E-HANDLE-ESCAPE`: a `ROOT` atom reaches a slot of `EXT`, an argument or a reachable slot of
  a call that resolves to a host call or is trapped (§4.5), or the return value of a function
  whose callers are not all in the link, other than a device entry's `QREG` result (§6.6). NS §3.1: "any handle use that survives into host native
  code is rejected".

H functions in the link that receive launch register ids through §6.6 are analysed like device code:
they may copy, store, compare and pass them back to device entries (§6.2 items 2–4); any other
use is `BD-E-HANDLE-USE`.

Handle provenance MUST flow through every indirect-call candidate's parameters and results exactly as through a direct defined call. [deps: DLT-5.2-P10, DLT-6.2-P5, DLT-6.4, ISA-3.3-P21, ISA-4.1-P11]

A tracked object containing handles MUST remain storable and returnable through other tracked objects, with escape decided by the reachable handle slots rather than object escape alone. [deps: DLT-5.2, DLT-6.4-P1, DLT-6.4-P2, ISA-7.1, ISA-3.3]

A global handle field MUST be analysed by reachable byte slots, allowing analysed host transport under the handle rules and retaining rejection only for an actual prohibited use or unknown-reader escape. [deps: DLT-5.2, DLT-6.4, DLT-6.6, ISA-3.3, ISA-10]

### 6.5 Realisation on ISA 0.8: static register files

ISA 0.8 has no instruction that stores, loads or converts a `qreg` (ISA §3.2, §3.3, §4.1), and
`qreg[n]` types have literal widths. CC code holds `qreg` values in its classical environment
(dynamic SSA instances, block parameters, call arguments and results; ISA §3.2, §7.4). The plugin
realises each allocation site s (a `qalloc` or `qborrow` call) in one of two ways:

Mixed-major bitcode links within the tested envelope MUST use its newest reader and matching plugin and library bitcode, retaining one target triple and DataLayout. [deps: DLT-19.8, DLT-21.1, ISA-9.1, ISA-9.2]

### 19.9 Options, identity, outputs, module flag

| Option | Meaning | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `-bennett-dialect=<v>` | MUST equal the plugin's dialect version (`BD-E-VERSION`) | required |
| `-bennett-isa=<v>` | MUST equal the ISA version the plugin targets (`BD-E-VERSION`) | `0.8` |
| `--bennett-budgets=<file>`, `--bennett-budget-default=<s,h,st,d,ss>` | §9.2 | §9.2 defaults |
| `--bennett-class=<name>` | §3.4 | none |
| `--bennett-tier=reversible`, `--bennett-entry=<sym>`, `--bennett-disk=<file>` | §19.2 | – |
| `--bennett-oracle=<sym>`, `--bennett-bind=…`, `--bennett-dev=…`, `--bennett-payload=…` | §19.3 | – |
| `--bennett-snapshot=<dump>` | §19.6 | – |
| `-bennett-closed-unit` | §19.7 | off |
| `--bennett-platform=<profile>` | §20 | none |
| `--bennett-vscale=<V>`, `--bennett-cpu=<model>` | §12.5, §15.2 | §24 |
| `--bennett-qreg-instances=<n>` | §6.5 | 64 |
| `-bennett-inplace-exhaustive-bits=<n>` | §8.7 | 24 |
| `-bennett-warn-qstore` | §6.7 | off |
| `-bennett-out=<dir>` | the outputs below | required |

**Identity.** `llvmGetPassPluginInfo` returns plugin name `bennett` and version string
`dialect-0.2.12;isa-0.8`; the driver refuses a plugin whose string differs. [deps: DLT-19.9, DLT-23] One plugin build per LLVM
major (NS §3.2).

**Outputs** (in `-bennett-out`, file names plugin-defined, §24 D22): the materialised device
variants as bitcode (§17.7); the host module with device entries replaced by launch stubs; the ISA
objects (ISA §9, §16); the certificate (ISA §12) with `producer.materialisation = {llvm_version,
pipeline, normaliser_version: "0.2.8"}` and `bindings.dialect_mapping_version = "0.2.9"`; the coverage
report (ISA §13), whose `source` field is `dialect-0.2.12:<rule id>` (e.g. `dialect-0.2.12:N33`); the
blocker reports (§25.1); and the **build record**, one JCS object with the build flags, the
budget table (resolved, including each shape's selection_identity, final semantic identity,
resolved components and frozen layout capacities under §9.2) and its SHA-256, the runtimes, arith and asm-table hashes, the snapshot
hash, the layout options and the `cpuid` leaf table (§15.2). Its SHA-256 is recorded as the build metadata of the certificate's
`producer.version` (`<driver version>+build.<hex>`) until the ISA certificate has a field for it
(§25.2). [deps: DLT-19.9, DLT-25.2]

**A missing plugin fails loud.** No production artefact defines a marker: `bennett-runtimes`,
`bennett-arith` and the driver's link inputs define no `__bennett_*` marker symbol (test runtimes
such as the trace runtime do, and are never linked into production). A link in which the plugin
did not run therefore leaves undefined references and fails at link time; the driver MUST NOT
ignore unresolved symbols or supply weak marker definitions.

**Module flag.** A producer MAY add `!{i32 1, !"bennett.dialect", !"0.2.12"}` (behaviour Error, so an
LTO link of modules of two dialect versions fails); the plugin MUST reject a module whose flag
differs from its version (`BD-E-VERSION`). [deps: DLT-19.9, DLT-23]

```llvm example=version-flag verdict=reject:BD-E-VERSION
define void @top() {
  %h = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 1)
  call void @__bennett_qfree(ptr %h)
  ret void
}

!llvm.module.flags = !{!0}
!0 = !{i32 1, !"bennett.dialect", !"0.1"}
```

# spec/

`Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md` is draft **0.2.12** (2026-11-01), from eight consumer-named gaps,
awaiting council review. It supersedes accepted draft 0.2.11 (round 10, sol/codex);
accepted 0.2.10 is retained in git, with binding interpretations in
[`errata-0.2.10.md`](errata-0.2.10.md). The reviewer disposition table is in
[`../docs/briefs/2026-11-01-dialect-0212-consumer-gaps.md`](../docs/briefs/2026-11-01-dialect-0212-consumer-gaps.md).
It targets
ISA 0.8 (`../bennett-isa-spec/spec/`, settled) and is graded against it. `bennett_markers.h` is the
normative C/C++ header of the marker ABI (section 3.3). `Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.2.PROGRESS.md` is
the writing job's log. Council records and the runner live in the `bennett` meta repo
(`docs/council/`, `tools/council.py`); the rubric is `docs/council/rubric-isa.md`, under which any
narrowing of north-star scope is S0.

Rules for changing it:

- **No narrowing** (CLAUDE.md rule 10). A compile-time rejection needs a row in section 22.1 citing
  the north-star clause or maintainer decision that requires it. A deferral (section 24) postpones
  mechanics only and states the capability it keeps. A capability ISA 0.8 cannot express is a
  blocker (section 25.1) with the exact ISA change and a `blocked:` verdict, never a rejection.
- Every semantic change bumps the full version string (header and section 23); drafts are not
  compatible with each other. The reference objects and hashes of section 16 depend on the ISA
  version string, the lifting map and the layout, so they change with any of them.
- **Checked examples** (section 1.5, process rules P-1 to P-3):
  - P-1: every rule id has an `accept` example, every rejection a `reject` example, every blocker
    a `blocked` example; Appendix B is the index, and a rule without one fails the check.
  - P-2: an example the checker cannot yet execute fails (`checker-unimplemented: <capability>`);
    no example is removed or weakened to make the checker pass; the freeze gate is all green.
  - P-3: executed results come from independent executors (LLVM tools, native binaries, the Rust
    ISA reference interpreter `bennett-isa-ref`); Python computes expected artefacts only.
- Run `python3 scripts/dialect_check_v02.py` before anyone grades this draft; it must exit 0. (For 0.2 the
  checker is being extended against the Rust interpreter in a separate job; until then the 0.2
  examples were executed by the writing job as its progress file records.)
- Add every new example or stated result as a tagged block, so the checker executes it; an
  untagged `llvm` or `isa` block is an error. A `semantics=` example must be byte-equal to its
  `corpus/legality/cases/spec-*.ll` file; run `python3 corpus/legality/harness/run.py baseline
  --majors 18,20,23 --cases 'cases/spec-*'` (headless, CLAUDE.md rule 11) after changing one.
- Write derivations out before stating numbers; every stated example result must be computed.
- Surgical edits only; a rewrite has introduced a mechanical error every time it was tried (ISA
  rounds 1-8).
- Revisions go back through the council.

spec/errata-0.2.10.md:33:The explicit exact-conversion and correct-rounding guarantees take priority over APFloat implementation fallbacks. PPCDoubleDouble denotes high+low, not the legacy consecutive-106-bit approximation. A low component may not be discarded when the exact sum is representable in the destination. Sparse-tail loss and intermediate unsigned-low saturation are not permitted by the named-format reference. Draft 0.2.12 §13.1 makes canonical-pair rounding and flags explicit; no claim that the current arith publication already meets these guarantees is made.
spec/errata-0.2.10.md:41:“Format named by metadata” means the LLVM major’s named interpretation MDString and its format/carrier semantics, not an unbounded user-created APFloat precision/exponent schema. LLVM 23 Intrinsics.td and LangRef define to(value, format, rounding, saturation) and from(bits, format). Draft 0.2.12 publishes NEW concrete carrier operand spellings and a static request/manifest protocol; those new ABI details are not claimed to have existed in 0.2.10.

spec/outline-0.3.md:937:Design consequence: the valid unsigned-i64 width/extent domain is finite even when the AV representation widens to top, so exhaustive static shape dispatch is a conservative construction, not a claim of efficient compilation. For a Co width, the padding bound is classical and fixed before entry; comparisons against the logical width remain coherent predicates. A more compact lowering is acceptable only with the same semantics; JIT object admission or a parametric region-signature extension is not assumed here. Invalid values and actual resource exhaustion retain the existing named outcomes. This replaces the obsolete top-width blocker case in 0.3; it does not silently transfer it to T-IFACE.
spec/outline-0.3.md:939:**Rejected alternatives:** Keeping the 4096-atom cap as a capability bound, substituting a user constant, or inspecting a superposed width classically are narrowings/errors. K1 does not by itself justify deleting the Co case without a construction.
spec/outline-0.3.md:943:**Dependency order:** I17, I33, I25; K1 publication/implementation in D01. The type-domain fallback can be extremely costly; staging is mechanics only.
spec/outline-0.3.md:952:**0.1 capability/history:** 0.1 rejected runtime budgets; the accepted version supports static and launch-supplied fields but not arbitrary device computations.
spec/outline-0.3.md:956:**ISA 0.9 provision:** K3: ISA-4.1-P17, ISA-5.4-P3 and ISA-7.4-P13 mint validated run-scoped tokens, revoke them on reversal and bind schedule/args to exact tuples.

8191:| `getelementptr T, ptr p, idx…` | Let P=P(a), I=I(a) for p's address space. Arithmetic on the low I bits is modulo 2^I; only representation bits above I are preserved, also for constant expressions. Canonical rows: acc is the pointer operand (when I<P, first `t = lift.trunc.i<I> P_operand` and acc is t). For each non-constant sequential index in index order: sign-extend/truncate to I (omit the cast when already I wide); if its element allocation size s≠1 emit `c = lift.const.i<I> (s mod 2^I)` then `t = lift.mul index c`, else use the index directly; emit `acc′ = lift.add acc t`. Constant sequential indices, interpreted signed at their source width then scaled by s, and constant structure field offsets accumulate into Δ mod 2^I, with no per-index rows. After the dynamic sequence, if Δ≠0 emit `c = lift.const.i<I> Δ` then `acc′ = lift.add acc c`. For I=P the last acc′ row writes v; with no dynamic index and Δ=0 the only row is `v = lift.mov P_operand`. For I<P always emit recombination, even when acc equals trunc(P_operand), in this order: `lo = lift.zext.i<P> acc`; `c = lift.const.i<P> <ones above I>`; `hi = lift.and P_operand c`; `v = lift.or hi lo`. Every emitted row is charged normally |
